# Tazkirah Awliya-e Pak o Hind — takeaways

*Source: ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن شارب (Dr Zahur ul-Hasan Sharib) — so the book's own footnotes name him in the third person (p. 45, folio 41; p. 67, folio 63, notes 10 and 15) and so he signs himself, "شارب", at the foot of the dedication (p. 9) and of the Persian petition-poem "مددے!" (p. 13); the notes do not quote the name as it stands on the title page, so the manifest's "(to confirm from title page)" is not yet discharged — تذکرہ اولیائے پاک و ہند on the cover (p. 1), "جدید تذکرۂ / تذکرہ اولیائے پاک و ہند" with the subtitle "خُم خانۂ تصوف / خُم خانۂ تصوّف", *Khum Khana-e Tasawwuf*, on both inner title pages (pp. 5, 7), and تذکرہ اولیاء پاک و ہند in the colophon (p. 8). Colophon (p. 8): first edition "بار اول | اکتوبر 1999ء", October 1999; print run "تعداد | 1100", 1,100 copies; printers "حاجی محمد حنیف پرنٹرز"; publishers "چوہدری غلام رسول" and "میاں جواد رسول"; price "80/=روپے"[OCR?]; "جملہ حقوق محفوظ ہیں" — but the preface is signed "۴، اپریل ۱۹۶۵ء [OCR?]", 4 April 1965, at Ajmer Sharif (p. 18), and the two years stand in conflict which this file does not resolve; the house whose name appears on the endpaper, the title page and the back cover is a different one again, "پروگریسو بکس", Progressive Books, اردو بازار, Lahore, printed in four non-agreeing address forms and with two non-agreeing telephone numbers (pp. 3, 7, 390, 392). 392 pages, transcribed by the `vision` route at render scale 1568 and assembled 15 September 2026; provenance in `out/ocr/tazkirah_awliya_pak_o_hind/`. Page references are PDF page indices; `folio` is the printed page number where the transcription records one. The relation is folio = PDF page index − 4, verified page by page from p. 21 (folio 17) to p. 388 (folio 384) with no drift recorded anywhere in the four consolidated ranges, and its only exceptions are omissions rather than shifts: pp. 1–20 and pp. 389–392 print no folio marker at all, and the blank pages p. 30, pp. 88 and 90, p. 144, pp. 202 and 204, pp. 246 and 248, pp. 310 and 312, pp. 344 and 346, p. 376 and p. 389 print none either, so folios 84, 86, 140, 198, 200, 242, 244, 306, 308, 340, 342, 372 and 385 never appear in the book's own numbering while the offset on both sides of every gap stays at 4.
Reviewed: no.*

*How this file was made: Pass 1 wrote twenty `chunk_NNN.notes.md` against the assembled transcription
(chunk size ~5000 words), kept in `entries/book_takeaways/tazkirah_awliya_pak_o_hind/`. Pass 2 ran as
four range consolidations — pp. 1–109 (chunks 001–005: the front matter, unfoliated pp. 1–20, plus
folios 17–105, باب 1 to باب 16 with باب 16 breaking off past the range); pp. 110–210 (chunks 006–010:
folios 106–206 less three `[blank page]` pages, باب 16 tail through باب 40 head, plus the dividers
حصہ چہارم and حصہ پنجم); pp. 211–303 (chunks 011–015, باب 41 to باب 58); pp. 304–392 (chunks 016–020,
باب 59 to باب 70 and the back matter) — which were then merged. Two archive rows, `data-darbar` and
`shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya`, were written about in more than one range and their sections are
merged across ranges; the other thirteen each sit wholly inside one range. The Cross-cutting, Figures,
Citable and Doubts sections are kept in range order rather than merged, so a reviewer can work one
range at a time against the page images. Nothing in this file has been reconciled that the book itself
leaves unreconciled.*

## What this book is

This is a compiled Urdu hagiographical compendium — a تذکرہ — of saints of what the book's own title calls پاک و ہند, Pakistan and India, and it tells the reader its own scope in a single sentence of the preface: "اس کتاب میں نہایت اختصار کے ساتھ ہفتاد یعنی ستر اولیائے کرام کے حالات", with the greatest brevity, the circumstances of *haftād*, that is seventy, awliya (p. 18). The claim is kept. The فہرست مضامین at pp. 15–16 prints باب ۱ through باب ۷۰, and the book ends where that count ends: باب 70, حضرت حاجی وارث علی شاہ, opens at p. 382 (folio 378) and its حواشی block closes at p. 388 (folio 384), the last printed folio in the volume. The seventy chapters are gathered above the chapter level into nine parts, حصہ اول to حصہ نہم, again as set out in the contents (pp. 15–16). The title itself is not stable: the cover prints تذکرہ اولیائے پاک و ہند (p. 1), both inner title pages print "جدید تذکرۂ / تذکرہ اولیائے پاک و ہند" with the subtitle "خُم خانۂ تصوف / خُم خانۂ تصوّف" (pp. 5, 7) — the word جدید, "New", and the subtitle appear nowhere else in the front matter — and the colophon prints تذکرہ اولیاء پاک و ہند (p. 8). Any citation of the title should carry the page of the form cited (pp. 1, 5, 7, 8). The archive's own record of the book is thinner than the book: the manifest short title is "Tazkirah Awliya e Pak o Hind", the author field reads "(to confirm from title page)", the year field is `null`, the group is `urdu_shrine`, the language field is `arabic` where the book as transcribed is Urdu carrying Persian quotation, the Drive file is titled "00523_Tazkirah-Awliya-e-Pak-o-Hind", and the manifest note reads "Compendium of saints across Pakistan and India; cross-cutting like the Tazkirah Awliya e Pakistan already used for Tier 2 entries."

The nine parts are marked in the body by divider pages, each of which carries a حصہ heading and a Persian couplet or quatrain and nothing else. Five of them are recorded in these notes: حصہ سوم at p. 89 (folio 85), with the quatrain "مرد خدا شاد بود زیر دلق / مرد خدا گنج بود در خراب // مرد خدا بحر بود بیکراں / مرد خدا قطرہ بود بے سحاب" (p. 89, folio 85); حصہ چہارم at p. 143 (folio 139); حصہ پنجم at p. 203 (folio 199); حصہ ششم at p. 247 (folio 243), with "وقت بچہ کار آید تسبیح و مرقع / خود را ز عمل ہائے نکوہیدہ بری دار // حاجت بکلاہ برکی [OCR?] داشت نیست / درویش صفت باش و کلاہ تتری دار" (p. 247, folio 243); حصہ ہفتم at p. 311 (folio 307), "گر تو سنگ خارۂ مرمر شوی / چوں بصاحب دل رسی گوہر شوی"; حصہ ہشتم at p. 345 (folio 341), "قال را بگذار مرد حال شو / پیش [OCR?] مرد کامل پائمال شو"; and حصہ نہم at p. 377 (folio 373), "دست مرد گیر تا مردے شوی / جز بمرداں نیست راہے رہبر". No divider page for حصہ اول or حصہ دوم is recorded anywhere in the four ranges — those two parts are attested by the contents list alone (pp. 15–16), and what page, if any, carries their dividers is (not stated). The dividers do not sit neatly on chapter boundaries: حصہ ہفتم falls between باب 59 and باب 60, so Part Seven opens at chapter 60 (p. 311, folio 307); حصہ ہشتم falls between باب 63 and باب 64, so Part Eight opens at chapter 64 (p. 345, folio 341); and حصہ نہم falls between باب 68 and باب 69 (p. 377, folio 373), which the notes for that range describe as falling "between two chapters, not at a boundary of its own". A researcher who wants to know which part a saint belongs to should therefore find the nearest preceding divider page rather than trust an even division of seventy by nine.

The chapter numbering runs cleanly except at one point, and the flaw is the book's, not the transcription's. باب 27 never appears, and "باب 28" is printed twice — once for حضرت شیخ احمد عبدالحق ردولوی at p. 169 (folio 165) and again for حضرت شاہ بدیع الدین مدار at p. 175 (folio 171) — and neither number carries an `[OCR?]` flag (pp. 169, 175; folios 165, 171). Before and after that the sequence is unbroken wherever a heading is visible: باب 1 opens at p. 21 (folio 17) and the openings of باب 1 to باب 16 are confirmed one by one against the contents list (pp. 15–16, 21, 33, 41, 43, 44, 46, 48, 56, 69, 72, 74, 77, 84, 89, 91, 97, 104); باب 16 to باب 40 run through pp. 110–210 (folios 106–206); باب 41 (p. 214, folio 210) to باب 58 (p. 303, folio 299) run unbroken, with the single caveat that the consolidation for that range infers an unnumbered entry at the head of one chunk "should be باب 50 in the preceding chunk" and states plainly that "this has not been verified here" (pp. 255, 260, 269; folios 251, 256, 265); and باب 59 (p. 306, folio 302) to باب 70 (p. 382, folio 378) are consecutive with "no break found at any chunk seam". Several entries are described in the notes only by place — the Kalpi entry (p. 305, folio 301), the unnamed entry at pp. 148–150 (folios 144–146), the unnamed Lahore entry whose fifteen حواشی are all to "سکینۃ اولیاء" (p. 277, folio 273), the Agra entry (p. 291, folio 287), the Bareilly entry (p. 362, folio 358), the Ganj Muradabad entry (p. 381, folio 377) — because their headings fall outside the chunk that transcribed their footnote block, not because the book leaves them unnamed.

Inside a chapter the book runs on a fixed grid of short labelled Urdu sub-headings, and the grid is applied very unevenly. The recurring labels across all four ranges are خاندانی حالات، والد (ماجد)، والدہ (ماجدہ)، بھائی، بہن، پیشین گوئی، ولادت (مبارک)، نام، لقب / القاب، کنیت، خطابات، تعلیم و تربیت، بیعت و خلافت، سیر و سیاحت، سلاطین سے تعلقات، ذریعہ معاش، عہدہ، شادی اور اولاد، وفات، خلفاء، سیرت، تعلیمات، اقوال، اوراد و وظائف / ورد و وظائف، مکشوفات و مقامات، کشف و کرامات (pp. 44, 51–52, 56–66, 69, 74–87, 93, folios 40, 47–48, 52–62, 65, 70–83, 89; pp. 113–119, folios 109–115; pp. 221–226, 236–240, folios 217–222, 232–236; pp. 313–321, 363–368, 382–386, folios 309–317, 359–364, 378–382). The density swings by an order of magnitude: باب 12 runs to twenty-odd headings over seven pages (pp. 77–83, folios 73–79) against باب 10's seven over two (pp. 72–73, folios 68–69); باب 25 uses some twenty-five headings across thirteen pages against باب 24's six across one (p. 151, folio 147); باب 44 fifteen over six pages (pp. 221–226, folios 217–222) against باب 43's four over one (p. 220, folio 216); باب 60 some twenty-five over nine pages (pp. 313–321, folios 309–317) against باب 59's fifteen over four (pp. 306–309, folios 302–305). All four ranges record the same interpretive rule in almost the same words, and it governs every "(not stated)" in this file: **the absence of a heading is the book's silence, not a gap in the transcription** (pp. 77–83, folios 73–79; p. 124, folio 120; pp. 133–141, folios 129–137; p. 151, folio 147; p. 188, folio 184; p. 220, folio 216; pp. 236–240, folios 232–236; pp. 255–257, folios 251–253; pp. 270–277, folios 266–273; pp. 313–321, folios 309–317; pp. 351–353, folios 347–349). The grid also takes on entry-specific headings where a story needs one — "زیارت حرمین شریف"، "فرمان"، "ارشاد عالی"، "آخری حج"، "مکن پور میں مستقل قیام"، "وصیت"، "مرتے کو ماریں زندہ شاہ مدار" in Shah Madar's chapter (pp. 175–181, folios 171–177); "کایا پلٹ"، "عطیہ"، "تاریخی یادگار" for Shaikh Sarang (pp. 182–183, folios 178–179); "شاہی دربار میں"، "قید و بند"، "رہائی"، "خبر جاں کاہ" (pp. 263–264, folios 259–260); "بچپن کے صدمات"، "عہدہ نظامت"، "اشارت پر بشارت"، "مہم میں شرکت"، "دوسرا خواب"، "تبدیلی"، "آگرہ کو روانگی"، "ترک دنیا"، "شرف زیارت"، "دربار غریب نواز میں" (pp. 281–285, folios 277–281) — and it widens markedly towards the end of the book, باب 68 adding doctrinal headings the rest of the book does not use (اسلام کی زندگی، غیر اللہ سے بے پروائی، توحید، سلوک، اوراد و وظائف, pp. 371–373, folios 367–369) and باب 70 adding بچپن کا صدمہ، بشارت، سیر و سیاحت، واپسی، سفر، حلقہ ارادت، اوراد و وظائف, which turns the last entry into "something close to a manual of practice" (pp. 382–386, folios 378–382). One heading, "کایا پلٹ" — the turn of life — is the compiler's own organising category for a conversion narrative and recurs six times in one range alone (pp. 217, 227, 234, 241, 251, 283; folios 213, 223, 230, 237, 247, 279), besides its appearance for Shaikh Sarang (pp. 182–183, folios 178–179). A caution on the grid: a heading does not guarantee its own content. "وفات" in باب 31 gives a mazar and no death date (p. 188, folio 184); "شادی اور اولاد" in باب 40 names three sons but no marriage and no wife (p. 209, folio 205); and "تخت سے دست برادری" is printed where "دست برداری" is what the section describes (p. 146, folio 142).

The other constant of the book's fabric is its footnote apparatus. Every chapter closes with its own numbered حواشی block, printed together at the chapter's end, and almost every substantive statement in the body carries a numbered marker: باب 1 has twenty notes at p. 29 (folio 25), باب 2 nineteen at p. 40 (folio 36), باب 7 nineteen at p. 55 (folio 51), باب 8 thirty-two at pp. 67–68 (folios 63–64), باب 15 thirteen at pp. 102–103 (folios 98–99), and the pattern holds through the rest of the volume (pp. 21–29, 48–52, 56–68, 74–87, 93–103; folios 17–25, 44–48, 52–64, 70–83, 89–99). This is, as the first range's notes put it, "a compiled, sourced compendium, not a narrative tazkirah". The attribution is at the level of the single fact: in باب 64, قوم, father, birth, name, laqab, bai'at, children, death and khalifas each carry their own marker (pp. 347–348, folios 343–344). Two structural consequences matter for use. First, entry length tracks the source and not the saint — باب 33 and باب 38 are single pages resting on a single citation, and باب 33 carries no footnote marker and no حواشی block at all (p. 191, folio 187). Second, because the chunking of the transcription cuts across chapters, several blocks are partial or orphaned: باب 45's block is cut off at p. 230 (folio 226) with only حواشی 1–2 printed while the text carries markers up to 14; باب 53's four markers have no block in their chunk (p. 269, folio 265); باب 55's seven markers point to a block that chunk never reaches (pp. 281–285, folios 277–281); باب 58's marker 1 has no block (p. 303, folio 299); and p. 231 (folio 227) carries a حواشی block and nothing else, items numbered 3 to 14, with no heading and no name, belonging to an entry that falls entirely before that chunk (p. 231, folio 227).

Everything the book says about its own author is in the front matter, pp. 7–18, and nothing at all after it: the consolidations for pp. 110–210, pp. 211–303 and pp. 304–392 each record independently that the compiler never names himself, gives no preface, no statement of method, no dedication and no self-characterisation in their pages — author's self-description in the body: **(not stated)** (pp. 110–210; pp. 211–303; pp. 304–392). In the front matter he prints his custodianship as his first credential, above his doctorate. The title page carries, under his name and degrees, "بانی صدر: دی سوسائٹی آف مسٹکس" — founder-president of The Society of Mystics — and then, on its own line, "سجادہ نشین", *sajjada nashin*, followed by two shrines: "حضرت مخدوم سماء الدین سہروردیؒ (مہرولی نئی دہلی)", Makhdum Sama al-Din Suhrawardi, Mehrauli, New Delhi, and "حضرت نواب گدڑی شاہ بابا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ (اجمیر شریف)", Nawab Gudri Shah Baba, Ajmer Sharif (p. 7). The compiler of this book is thus a serving custodian holding two Indian sajjada nashini seats at once, not an outside historian. His address is given as "شارب انسٹی ٹیوٹ آف اسلامک لٹریچر، شارب ہاؤس، جھالرہ، اجمیر شریف" — the Sharib Institute of Islamic Literature, Sharib House, جھالرہ, Jhalra, Ajmer Sharif (p. 18). His pen-name and signature is "شارب", Sharib (pp. 9, 13). His full name reaches the reader chiefly through his own footnotes, in the third person: "'معین الہند' از ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن شارب ص 111، 191" (p. 45, folio 41); "دلی کے (22) بائیس خواجہ از ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن شارب ص 38" (p. 67, folio 63, note 10); "'معین الہند'[OCR?] از ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن شارب ص 98" (p. 67, folio 63, note 15); and once more, far away at the back, in a note recording that "رباعیات سرمد کا انگریزی میں ترجمہ ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن شارب نے کیا ہے" — an English translation of the *Rubaiyat-e Sarmad* made by Dr Zahur ul-Hasan Sharib (p. 302, folio 298, حاشیہ 7). The book is dedicated to حضرت خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کاکیؒ, titled قطب الاقطاب: "کی بارگاہ میں یہ چند اوراق بصد عجز و نیاز پیش کرتا ہوں", I present these few leaves at his court with all humility, closing "گر قبول افتد زہے عز و شرف" (p. 9).

The preface also states the book's place in a series and — decisively for how the archive may use it — its criterion of inclusion, which is negative. "میں نے 'معین الہند' لکھ کر خواجہ خواجگان حضرت خواجہ معین الدین حسن چشتیؒ کو خراج تحسین پیش کیا۔ پھر 'دلی کے بائیس خواجہ' لکھ کر دلی کے برگزیدہ پیران عظام[OCR?] کو نذر عقیدت پیش کی۔ یہ کتاب اس سلسلے کی تیسری کڑی ہے" — *Muin al-Hind* as tribute to Khwaja Moinuddin Hasan Chishti, then *Dilli ke Bais Khwaja*, "Delhi's twenty-two Khwajas"; this book is the third link in that chain (p. 18). And then: "اس کتاب میں ان اولیائے کرام کے حالات شامل ہیں جن کے حالات 'معین الہند' اور 'دلی کے بائیس خواجہ' میں جگہ نہ پا سکے" — it contains the circumstances of those awliya whose circumstances could not find a place in the first two books (p. 18). The coverage of this volume is therefore the residue of two earlier volumes and not a survey, so **an absence from this book is not evidence of anything**. He also claims priority without argument — "یہ کتاب اپنی نوعیت کی پہلی کوشش ہے", this book is the first attempt of its kind — and names the reward he seeks as the saints' acceptance rather than the reader's: "اگر میری یہ کتاب ان اولیائے کرام نے قبول فرمائی تو یہ میری محنت کا بہترین صلہ ہوگا" (p. 18). What he promises for each saint is worth quoting in full, because of what it leaves out: "حالات طیبات، خوارق عادات، غرائب و اقعات و حکایت[OCR?]، نادر اسرافات[OCR?]، ترک لذات، مراقبات و مشاہدات، اور ورد و وظائف و معمولات، کلمات طیبات و تعلیمات، کشف و کرامات، مجاہدات و عبادات و ریاضات، مکشوفات اور مقامات" (p. 18). Nothing in that list is a shrine, an urs, a custodianship or an endowment. The author's own programme is biographical and devotional, not institutional, and the Hujwiri entry bears it out by giving the mazar a single sentence (p. 23, folio 19).

In the body his voice is heavily constrained and almost entirely editorial. He speaks unsourced in two registers only: the one-line encomium that opens each chapter — "حضرت شیخ رکن الدین ابو الفتح خورشید سپہر ہدایت ہیں" (p. 113, folio 109), "حضرت قاضی شہاب الدین دولت آبادی باکمال درویش تھے" (p. 185, folio 181), "حضرت شاہ مینا واقف رموز ربانی ہیں" (p. 192, folio 188) — and the occasional extended gloss, as on the proverb "مرتے کو ماریں زندہ شاہ مدار", where he remarks "اس کے معنی' مضمرات اور متعلقات سے بہت ہی کم لوگ واقف ہیں", very few know its meaning, implications and connections, and then supplies the meaning himself (p. 178, folio 174). He marks unsourced report with a standing formula and uses it consistently: "یہ کہا جاتا ہے کہ" (p. 130, folio 126), "آپ کے متعلق یہ کہا جاتا ہے کہ" (p. 134, folio 130), "یہ بھی کہا جاتا [as printed]" (p. 150, folio 146), "یہ کہا جاتا ہے" and "مجرب بتایا جاتا ہے" (p. 174, folio 170), "کہا جاتا ہے کہ" (p. 206, folio 202). His standing editorial habit is to print variants side by side and to choose neither: on sama, "سماع سنتے تھے۔ بعض کہتے ہیں کہ سماع نہیں سنتے تھے" (p. 137, folio 133, حاشیہ 11); Jamali's original name and takhallus, the second introduced "یہ بھی کہا جاتا ہے" and separately footnoted (p. 221, folio 217, حواشی 1–2); Abdul Quddus's father under "بعض کے نزدیک" (p. 227, folio 223, حواشی 2–3) and his death year, "بعض نے آپ کی وفات 945ھ میں ہونا لکھا ہے" (p. 228, folio 224); Shaikh Salim Chishti's birth year, the range's one explicit اختلاف, "آپ کے سن ولادت میں اختلاف ہے' بعض نے آپ کا سن ولادت 897ھ لکھا ہے" (p. 236, folio 232); Abul Muali's birth, "یہ بھی کہا جاتا ہے کہ 10- ذو الحجہ 920ھ کو پیدا ہوئے" (p. 255, folio 251); two accounts of one bai'at in باب 62, "آپ کی حضرت شیخ کلیم اللہ شاہ جہاں آبادی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ سے بیعت ہونے کی تفصیل اس طرح بھی بیان کی جاتی ہے", the two differing on the trigger, on whether Shaikh Yahya Madani was alive, and on what was being read (p. 334, folio 330); two reasons for one journey in باب 65, "ہندوستان میں آپ کے تشریف لانے کی دو وجوہات بتائی جاتی ہیں" (p. 351, folio 347); and even the title مجدد الف ثانی, given only as "یہ کہا جاتا ہے" (p. 261, folio 257). He records a wonder and its correction with equal weight and adjudicates neither: the dry-shod river crossing is followed immediately by the pir's "اسباب ظاہر کی رعایت ضروری ہے" and by a return crossing made "کشتی سے", by boat (pp. 327–328, folios 323–324), and the beard-hair kashf is answered with "میران! یہ فقر نہیں ہے' فقر دوسری چیز ہے" (p. 327, folio 323). He prints an internal tension without remarking on it — "آپ لکھے پڑھے نہیں تھے' اس کے باوجود آپ بہت سی کتابوں کے مصنف ہیں", Sultan Bahu was unlettered and nonetheless the author of many books, followed at once by twenty-four titles and two divans (pp. 316–317, folios 312–313). He softens a source once, visibly, turning the claim that a saint absorbed whatever gift a person had into a social explanation by the book's own "یعنی" (pp. 134–135, folios 130–131, حاشیہ 3). He makes exactly one evaluative recommendation in the last quarter of the book, of باب 68: "آپ کی تعلیمات اہل ذوق و شوق اہل تصوف اور اہل عرفان ہی کے لئے مفید نہیں ہیں' بلکہ ہر شخص آپ کی تعلیمات سے خاطر خواہ فائدہ اٹھا سکتا ہے" (p. 371, folio 367). And in one span only — the chunk covering pp. 286–303 — does he reason in his own voice at all: he stages a disagreement among three named tazkirah-writers over where Sarmad's love episode occurred and adjudicates none of them, "تذکرہ نویسوں میں اختلاف ہے کہ یہ واقعہ کہاں ہوا" (p. 298, folio 294); he passes judgement on a legal proceeding, "آپ پر جو فرد جرم لگائی گئی' وہ سیاسی آمیزش سے خالی نہ تھی", the charge-sheet laid against him was not free of political admixture (p. 298, folio 294); and he supplies a demographic note of his own, "ایران میں ارمنیوں کی کافی تعداد تھی' جس میں سے کچھ عیسائی تھے اور کچھ یہودی" (p. 297, folio 293). Otherwise, in the words of three of the four consolidations, nothing in the book is offered as the compiler's own inquiry and he does not argue with a source anywhere (pp. 110–210; p. 226, folio 222; pp. 233–245, folios 229–241; pp. 255–268, folios 251–264; pp. 277–280, folios 273–276; pp. 304–392).

The book names no oral informant anywhere in the four ranges. Its authorities are printed books, named in the حواشی, and the base is Persian classics, Urdu translations of them, saint-specific monographs, and twentieth-century Urdu secondary writing including the compiler's own two earlier volumes, all cited without any mark distinguishing their authority (pp. 29, 40, 45, 47, 55, 67–68, 83, 87, 102–103; folios 25, 36, 41, 43, 51, 63–64, 79, 83, 98–99). The recurring workhorses across the whole volume are اخبار الاخیار (اردو ترجمہ), cited from p. 35 (folio 31) to p. 243 (folio 239) and in one chunk "effectively the compiler's only source"; سالک السالکین (جلد دوم), which alone carries twelve of باب 7's nineteen notes (p. 55, folio 51) and most of باب 17 and باب 20 (p. 120, folio 116; p. 126, folio 122); سیر الاقطاب (فارسی), from which all twelve of باب 21's حواشی come (p. 132, folio 128); سفینۃ الاولیاء (فارسی); سیر الاولیاء (فارسی); کشف المحجوب (اردو[OCR?] ترجمہ); کشف الاسرار (اردو ترجمہ), to which nine of باب 1's twenty notes point (p. 29, folio 25); اخبار/خیر المجالس, تاریخ فرشتہ (فارسی), راحت القلوب, انوار العارفین (فارسی) — "the single largest authority" in the final range (pp. 331, 350, 353, 368; folios 327, 346, 349, 364) — تکملہ سیر الاولیاء (فارسی), تذکرۃ العابدین and معارج الولایت. Where a saint has a dedicated monograph the compiler leans on it almost to the exclusion of everything else: مدار اعظم supplies nine of Shah Madar's sixteen حواشی (pp. 176–181, folios 172–177); لطائف اشرفی carries the unnamed entry at pp. 148–150 (p. 150, folio 146); all fifteen notes of the unnamed Lahore entry are to سکینۃ اولیاء (p. 277, folio 273); all eighteen of باب 68 are to سیرت غوثیہ (p. 375, folio 371); all twenty-four of باب 70 are to حیات وارث, running ص 224 to ص 360 (pp. 387–388, folios 383–384). He also cites a subject's own books as the authority for that subject's teaching — معارف لدنیہ (اردو ترجمہ), مبدو معار[OCR?] (اردو ترجمہ) and مکتوب 197 for باب 52 (p. 268, folio 264); نظام القلوب for باب 62 (p. 336, folio 332; p. 339, folio 335); عین الفقر, گنج الاسرار (اردو ترجمہ) and کشف الاسرار (اردو ترجمہ) for the teaching half of باب 60, whose biographical half is footnoted separately to مناقب سلطانی (p. 321, folio 317) — so that the doctrinal sections of those entries are quotation from the saint rather than the compiler's characterisation. Exactly one source in the book is cited by its holding institution: "ریاض الشعراء (قلمی نسخہ اشیاٹک[OCR?] سوسائٹی آف بنگال کے کتب خانہ میں موجود ہے۔)", a manuscript copy present in the library of the Asiatic [OCR?] Society of Bengal (p. 301, folio 297). The canon widens as the book proceeds: Persian tazkirah staples dominate the earlier chapters, by باب 52 he is quoting his subject's own books page by page (p. 268, folio 264), and by باب 57 he is weighing three named tazkirah-writers against each other (p. 298, folio 294).

Two features of that source base demand caution. First, the printed forms of the titles are unstable, and the instability is in the print as often as in the OCR: فوائد الفوائد appears once as فوائد القوائد (p. 67, folio 63, note 19); سیر الاقطاب once as سیر الاقطب (p. 83, folio 79); روضۃ الاقطاب and روضۃ اقطاب both occur (pp. 67–68, 83, 102–103; folios 63–64, 79, 98–99); معارج الولایت (pp. 111–112, 150; folios 107–108, 146) and معراج الولایت (pp. 169, 174; folios 165, 170) are both printed; لطائف اشرفی appears in four forms of its title (p. 150, folio 146; pp. 166, 178, 181; folios 162, 174, 177); ذوالفقار بدیع, ذو والفقار بدیع and ذو الفقار بدیع are three spellings of one title (pp. 180, 181; folios 176, 177); خزینۃ الاصفیاء (p. 21, folio 17) reappears as خیزینۃ الاصفیا[OCR?] (p. 226, folio 222); اخبار الاخیار appears once as اخبار اخیار (p. 231, folio 227); مناقب المحجوبین [OCR?] and مناقب محجوبین[OCR?] are two flagged, pageless forms of one title (p. 350, folio 346; p. 368, folio 364); and one title simply does not read, "اسیر اولالیاء [OCR?]" (فارسی) ص 185 (p. 96, folio 92). **The four inputs disagree with each other on one such title and the disagreement is left open here**: the work cited through most of the book as سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) (pp. 36–37, 40, 53–55, 75–76, 111–112, 120, 126, 142, 152, 201; folios 32–33, 36, 49–51, 71–72, 107–108, 116, 122, 138, 148, 197) is cited at p. 381 (folio 377) as مسالک السالکین جلد دوم, and the notes for that range state expressly that neither form is OCR-flagged in its chunk. Both forms stand. Note also that کشف الاسرار is cited as a source for باب 1 (pp. 22–29, folios 18–25) and separately as one of Sultan Bahu's own works in باب 60 (p. 321, folio 317); the inputs do not say whether these are the same book. Second, citations are sometimes defective in ways that carry no flag: "اخبار الاخیار (اردو ترجمہ 234" is printed with no "ص" before the page number (p. 190, folio 186); سیر العارفین is cited twice with no page at all and once run together with another title as "سیر العارفین سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 427" (pp. 120, 142; folios 116, 138); four notes read only "مکتوب" (pp. 111–112, folios 107–108); حاشیہ 4 of باب 64 gives مناقب المحجوبین [OCR?] alone, with no page, where every other note in its block carries one (p. 350, folio 346).

The edition is the least settled thing about this book, and the archive should treat it as unresolved. The colophon at p. 8 prints a first edition, "بار اول | اکتوبر 1999ء", October 1999, with a print run of "تعداد | 1100", 1,100 copies, printers "حاجی محمد حنیف پرنٹرز", publishers "چوہدری غلام رسول" and "میاں جواد رسول", a price of "80/=روپے"[OCR?] and the rights line "جملہ حقوق محفوظ ہیں": a modern in-copyright print. It names two points of sale, "اسلام بکڈپو دوکان نمبر 12 گنج بخش روڈ لاہور", Islam Book Depot, shop no. 12, Ganj Bakhsh Road, Lahore, and "ملت پبلی کیشنز۔ فیصل مسجد اسلام آباد", Millat Publications, Faisal Masjid, Islamabad (p. 8). Against that, the preface is signed "۴، اپریل ۱۹۶۵ء [OCR?]", 4 April 1965, at Ajmer Sharif (p. 18). **The two dates are in conflict and this file does not resolve the conflict.** The last range, which reaches the back of the book, states explicitly that it cannot adjudicate between the preface's 1965 and the colophon's 1999, and establishes only the negative point that the 1999 statement cannot be a back-of-book colophon and must sit in the front matter (pp. 390–392). Layered on top of that is a publisher problem. The names in the colophon (چوہدری غلام رسول, میاں جواد رسول, حاجی محمد حنیف پرنٹرز, p. 8) are not the house name that appears elsewhere in the volume, which is پروگریسو بکس, Progressive Books, and the inputs do not reconcile them. Progressive Books appears in four non-agreeing address forms: the endpaper stamp reads "پروگریسو بکس [OCR?]   ۲۴ بی اردو بازار لاہور [OCR?]" with the number "7352795" (p. 3); the title-page imprint reads "پروگریسو بکس ۳۰-بی، اُردو بازار، لاہور [OCR?]" with "فون: ۶۳۵۲۶۹۵ [OCR?]" (p. 7); the endpaper device at the back, transcribed once from a tiled image, reads "پروگریسو بکس / بہاری [OCR?] اردو بازار لاہور / 7352795", with the telephone number not flagged (p. 390); and the back cover reads "پروگریسو بکس ○ 40 [OCR?] بی اردو بازار ○ لاہور", with both the street-or-house element and the number carrying `[OCR?]` (p. 392). Two telephone numbers stand — 7352795 (pp. 3, 390) and ۶۳۵۲۶۹۵ [OCR?] (p. 7) — and the notes for the first range record expressly that "the two addresses and the two telephone numbers do not agree". The copy itself carries two marks of ownership: a handwritten ink inscription across the upper half of the inner title page, "فقیر عبد ال[illegible] دوم [illegible] نقشبندی [OCR?]", from which the owner's name is not recoverable (p. 5), and a handwritten number at an angle in the upper corner of the same page, "28.0102 [OCR?]", which resembles an accession or shelf number but is not identified as one (p. 5). The back matter is a publisher's endpaper and a sales catalogue: pp. 390–392 are transcribed from page images the transcriber describes rather than reproduces — a tiled "[image: publisher's endpaper — a dome-and-minaret silhouette with a logo panel…]" at p. 390 and again at p. 391, where the transcriber notes "no imprint line on this side", and at p. 392 "[image: back cover — a publisher's advertisement drawn as a three-dimensional box, its two faces carrying the book list in two columns, hand-lettered in Nastaliq]". The wording inside the repeated device was transcribed once, on the transcriber's statement that "the device below carries the same wording each time" (p. 390), which means **a human verifying the imprint must look at the images**. The back cover lists eighteen titles under "ہمارے ہاں مذہبی دینی اور عارفانہ کلام کی خوبصورت ترین کتابیں دستیاب ہیں' آج ہی آئیں اور اپنی مطلوبہ کتب خرید فرمائیں" and prints no prices (p. 392).

What the book does not have is as important as what it has. There is no colophon at the back of this volume at all: pp. 390–392 carry no press name, no place of printing, no year, no edition statement, no print run and no patron (pp. 390–392). There is no کتابیات, no مآخذ, no فہرست or index and no errata anywhere in the back matter, and since p. 388 (folio 384) is the last printed folio, the book has none at the back at all — the chain of transmission is carried only in the per-chapter حواشی (pp. 387–392). No publisher's rights line, price line, digitisation stamp or library watermark appears anywhere after p. 8: the consolidations for pp. 35–109, pp. 110–210, pp. 211–303 and pp. 304–379 each record the same negative independently for their own pages, and each records that the pages they transcribed are body text, verse, sub-headings, chapter headings, حواشی blocks and part dividers only. There are no oral informants anywhere. And, with two classes of exception, there are no Common-Era dates: the front matter prints the CE years 1999 (p. 8) and ۱۹۶۵ (p. 18), and in the body the compiler converts Hijri to CE exactly once in the whole of the last range, for Haji Waris Ali Shah's two dates, printed as Hijri "مطابق" CE (p. 383, folio 379). Every other date in that range, and every date reported from the other three, is Hijri only. **That selectivity is itself a fact about how this book's dates were produced**, and it is the reason no Hijri year in this file is converted.

The folio-to-PDF-page relation is the one thing in this book that is entirely stable. The arithmetic is folio = PDF page index − 4, that is PDF page = folio + 4, and all four consolidations verify it independently across their own ranges with no exception recorded anywhere: pp. 21–29 → folios 17–25, p. 31 → folio 27, pp. 33–34 → folios 29–30, p. 35 → folio 31 through p. 52 → folio 48 with "every marker in between reading exactly that", p. 53 → folio 49 through p. 70 → folio 66, p. 71 → folio 67 through p. 92 → folio 88, p. 93 → folio 89 through p. 109 → folio 105; pp. 110–128 → folios 106–124, pp. 129–147 → folios 125–143, pp. 148–165 → folios 144–161 across all eighteen pages, pp. 166–184 → folios 162–180 across all nineteen, pp. 185–210 → folios 181–206; p. 211 → folio 207 through p. 230 → folio 226, p. 231 → folio 227 through p. 252 → folio 248, and the relation stated outright for pp. 253–269, pp. 270–285 and pp. 286–303; and in the last range p. 304 → folio 300, p. 324 → folio 320, p. 325 → folio 321, p. 340 → folio 336, p. 341 → folio 337, p. 359 → folio 355, p. 360 → folio 356, p. 379 → folio 375, p. 380 → folio 376, and p. 388 → folio 384, the last printed folio in the book. It breaks in three places and in three ways, none of them a drift. First, the front matter: pp. 1–20 carry no `[folio N]` marker at all, so the title pages, the endpaper stamp, the colophon, the dedication, the two poems, the contents and the preface are citable by PDF page only (pp. 1–20). Second, the back matter: pp. 390–392 print no folio at all (pp. 390–392). Third, blank pages print no marker, so folio numbers are consumed and never appear: p. 30 is blank (chunk 001, pp. 21–34); pp. 88 and 90 are blank, so folios 84 and 86 never appear; p. 144 is blank, so folio 140 never appears; pp. 202 and 204, so folios 198 and 200 never appear; pp. 246 and 248, so folios 242 and 244 never appear; pp. 310 and 312, so folios 306 and 308 never appear; pp. 344 and 346, so folios 340 and 342 never appear; p. 376, so folio 372 never appears; and p. 389, so folio 385 never appears. In every case the offset on both sides of the gap is unchanged — p. 247 → folio 243 and p. 249 → folio 245 straddle one such hole, p. 377 → folio 373 straddles another — so the printed folio sequence has holes while the arithmetic does not. Two further points follow. The contents list's printed page numbers are folios, not PDF indices, and the relation converts them exactly: باب ۱ → ۱۷ = p. 21, باب ۲ → ۲۹ = p. 33, باب ۳ → ۳۷ = p. 41, باب ۴ → ۳۹ = p. 43, باب ۵ → ۴۰ = p. 44, باب ۶ → ۴۲ = p. 46, باب ۷ → ۴۴ = p. 48, باب ۸ → ۵۲ = p. 56, باب ۹ → ۶۵ = p. 69, باب ۱۰ → ۶۸ = p. 72, باب ۱۱ → ۷۰ = p. 74, باب ۱۲ → ۷۳ = p. 77, باب ۱۳ → ۸۰ = p. 84, حصہ سوم → ۸۵ = p. 89, باب ۱۴ → ۸۷ = p. 91, باب ۱۵ → ۹۳ = p. 97, باب ۱۶ → ۱۰۰ = p. 104 — sixteen independent confirmations and no failures (pp. 15–16 against pp. 21, 33, 41, 43, 44, 46, 48, 56, 69, 72, 74, 77, 84, 89, 91, 97, 104). And the relation creates one trap that must never be sprung: **PDF page 17 and printed page 17 are different pages**. The contents sends باب ۱ to printed ۱۷ (p. 15); printed 17 is the `[folio 17]` marker at PDF p. 21; PDF p. 17 is the first page of the دیباچہ in the unfoliated front matter (pp. 15, 17, 21).

The material is not evenly distributed against the archive's interests, and the shape of that distribution follows from the author's programme rather than from any accident of the scan. Because the promised categories at p. 18 are biographical and devotional and contain no shrine, urs, custodianship or endowment, and because the Hujwiri entry gives its mazar one sentence (p. 23, folio 19), the early part of the book yields very little institutional material. The first range, pp. 1–109 (folios 17–105, where foliated), engages no archive row in its notes at all: what it contributes is the front matter — title, author, offices, series, criterion, colophon, imprint, preface date — and the method of the book, not entries. The second range, pp. 110–210 (folios 106–206), likewise names no archive row; it records ten shrine locations across its twenty-five chapters and flags five of the ten (pp. 110–210), which is a caution about place-names rather than a set of usable rows. The explicit archive engagements named anywhere in these four notes are three, and all fall in the second half of the book: the silsila word "ملامتیہ[OCR?]", "the very word that agrees with an archive row's silsila", at p. 251 (folio 247); the pir's town "اینتھہ [OCR?]", "on which an archive exclusion turns", at pp. 326–328 (folios 322–324); and the Taunsa subject's birthplace "گڑ گوجی[OCR?]" at p. 363 (folio 359), described as "the one datum that would corroborate his archive row independently". All three are OCR-flagged words, which is the single most important thing this introduction has to say about the book's usefulness. Alongside them, a fourth locational sentence is compromised: "لاہو [OCR?]" beside an unnamed موضع, in the sentence that locates a mazar (p. 279, folio 275). The remaining distributional facts worth carrying are that the book's chronological centre of gravity moves forward as it goes — the death years reported in the first range are Hujwiri's "465ھ" and "456ھ" (p. 23, folio 19), those in the second run from "9 رمضان 649ھ" (p. 113, folio 109) to "19 جمادی الثانی 921ھ" (p. 210, folio 206), and those in the last from "996ھ" (p. 306, folio 302) to "9 محرم الحرام 1323ھ" and "30 محرم الحرام 1323ھ" (pp. 382–383, folios 378–379) — and that the last chapters, باب 68 and باب 70, carry the book's densest doctrinal and practice material (pp. 371–373, 382–386; folios 367–369, 378–382). One document is reproduced in full rather than summarised: a Persian letter of Nizam al-Din to Fakhr al-Din Maruzi on "محبت رب العالمین", printed with a facing Urdu translation under the rubric "ترجمہ" (pp. 121–122, folios 117–118) — with the warning, recorded in the notes for that range, that the printed Urdu does not render all of the printed Persian.

**OCR and dating cautions. Three classes of reading in this book are unreliable and must not be quoted or carried without inspection of the page image. (i) Verse is the worst-damaged category throughout and should not be quoted as text anywhere in the volume: the Persian غزل attributed to Hujwiri has five of its seven rhyme words flagged — "در ولا [OCR?]", "مست ہلا [OCR?]", "ہم جتلا [OCR?]", "ہرگز تولا [OCR?]", "ہر سو صلا [OCR?]" — and its refrain differs between flagged and unflagged lines (p. 24, folio 20); Bu Ali Qalandar's مناجات carries nineteen flags and one `[illegible]` (pp. 101–102, folios 97–98); six ghazals, ruba'is, qit'as and couplets in pp. 110–210 carry between two and five flags each (pp. 143, 148, 159, 173, 185, 193, 200, 203; folios 139, 144, 155, 169, 181, 189, 196, 199); six of the eight Persian lines in praise of Abd al-Qadir Jilani are flagged (p. 317, folio 313), five of the ten lines of the پیم کہانی (p. 308, folio 304), five at p. 358 (folio 354), three in the mazar-door chronogram — "بیکشنبہ[OCR?]", "گرای[OCR?]", "سرورش[OCR?]" (p. 352, folio 348) — and one in the حصہ ہشتم couplet itself, "پیش [OCR?]" (p. 345, folio 341). (ii) Personal names, place-names, khalifa and murid lists, genealogies and lists of practices are the densest risk, and the flags land precisely on the fields a shrine entry needs: subjects' own names ("حضرت خواجہ خانو[OCR?]" flagged in both occurrences, p. 220, folio 216; "مجھن[OCR?]", p. 195, folio 191; "آپ کا نام نامی راز احمد[OCR?] ہے", p. 354, folio 350), shrine and home towns ("دہکلی[OCR?]", pp. 167–168, folios 163–164; "سرکھیج[OCR?]", p. 188, folio 184; "نزہر [OCR?]" flagged every time it is printed, pp. 234–235, folios 230–231; "بجے پور [OCR?]" flagged on both occurrences, p. 383, folio 379), silsila and doctrinal terms ("چشتیہ جمانیہ[OCR?]", p. 191, folio 187; "طریقہ قادریہ جدیہ[OCR?]", p. 263, folio 259; "اولیسی[OCR?]", p. 178, folio 174), and whole lists — seven of the thirty-eight items of باب 66's اذکار و اشغال list (p. 359, folio 355) and fourteen of the eighteen items of its "دیگر اشغال" list (p. 360, folio 356). (iii) A quieter risk than the flags: unflagged corruption, which must not be silently corrected and must not be mistaken for clean text — "چار قرض" for "چار قرص" inside one karamat (p. 119, folio 115), "خلفائے راشیدین" (p. 148, folio 144), "نمازہ جنازہ" (p. 150, folio 146), "شادی اور اولا" (p. 172, folio 168), "قاضی شیخ قوم الدین دہلوی" respelled "قوام الدین" in the next sentence (p. 191, folio 187), a duplicated clause (p. 267, folio 263), a heading and its whole block printed twice (p. 281, folio 277), "فتح پر سیکری" for "فتح پور سیکری" in the sentence that locates a mazar (p. 238, folio 234), "گوالیا" for "گوالیار" (p. 268, folio 264), "والد ماجدہ" in the feminine for a father (p. 262, folio 258). On dates specifically the four inputs do not agree, and both positions stand: the notes for pp. 110–210 find that "not one death date, birth date or numeral in the range carries an `[OCR?]` flag" and that "dates and counts in this range can be carried with confidence in the reading (though not in the book's accuracy)", while the notes for pp. 1–109 find the numeral sets mixed between Urdu and Western digits, cannot tell whether the book or the transcription mixes them, and rule that "any numeral quoted from this book should be checked against the page image" (pp. 3, 7–8, 15–18, 23, 29; folios 19, 25). The standing instruction for this archive follows, and it is not negotiable: **no date from this book may be used without checking the page image, and no date from this book may be cited without saying which of the two edition years — the preface's ۴، اپریل ۱۹۶۵ء [OCR?] (p. 18) or the colophon's بار اول اکتوبر 1999ء (p. 8) — is meant by "this edition". No Hijri year here is to be converted to a Common-Era year, and no age or interval is to be computed that the book does not itself print; the book converts Hijri to CE exactly once, at p. 383 (folio 379), and that single conversion is the book's, not ours.**

Two further transcription artefacts should be carried with any quotation. The Urdu comma is rendered inconsistently: as an apostrophe in some chunks ("16' رجب 735ھ", "سیر الاولیاء ص 298'299", pp. 110–128, folios 106–124) and as "،" in others ("16، رجب 736ھ", p. 155, folio 151; "5، شوال 731ھ", p. 154, folio 150), so both conventions occur inside a single range and a quotation should be taken from the page cited rather than normalised. And `[illegible]` is rare but real: once in pp. 1–109, in the phrase "چنڈول پر سوار داخل [illegible] میں چنڈول سے اترے", where the place in the khanqah at which he dismounted is lost and that is the very point at issue in the quarrel (p. 78, folio 74), besides the unreadable owner's inscription at p. 5; once in pp. 110–210, "و ہرچہ مانع شرط است [illegible] مشروط است" in the Persian letter (p. 121, folio 117); three times in pp. 211–303, a short superscript mark after a footnote number (p. 217, folio 213) and two marginal marks immediately above حواشی blocks which the notes say may be page-furniture — a catchword, signature mark or running head — rather than text (p. 277, folio 273; p. 280, folio 276); and three times in pp. 304–392, "ص[illegible]" inside a heading (p. 320, folio 316), "مصدر [illegible] یزدانی [OCR?]" in باب 62's opening line (p. 332, folio 328), and "آپ [illegible] شاہ بھاول کا مشورہ قبول کیا" (p. 326, folio 322). One scan defect is recorded: "[last line clipped at foot of scan]" at p. 206 (folio 202), which loses the head of Khwaja Husain Nagauri's book-list.

## Shrines in the archive this book informs

Fifteen archive rows are engaged across the book's 392 pages, ordered here by the weight of material. Two of them — `data-darbar` and `shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya` — are written about in more than one range and their sections are merged from those ranges; where the ranges disagree, or where one treats a figure at chapter length and another in a single clause, the section says so rather than smoothing it. The remaining thirteen each fall wholly inside one range.

Two range-level notes belong here before the entries.

- **pp. 110–210.** Four archive ids are engaged in this range, all by chunks 006 and 007. **Chunks 008, 009 and 010
  (pp. 148–210) engage no archive row at all**, and each of those chunk notes records that as a
  decision rather than an omission: every mazar located on those sixty-three pages is placed by the book
  at محمد پور، ظفر آباد، دہلی، دولت آباد، خلد آباد، گوالیار، چندیری، بڑودہ، کھمبائت، گلبرگہ، دہکلی[OCR?]،
  مکن پور، لکھوء[OCR?]، جونپور، سرکھیج[OCR?]، بتوہ[OCR?]، احمد آباد، بہرائچ، لکھنؤ، خیر آباد، مانکپور،
  فیض آباد، ناگور or کتھیل[OCR?] (p. 156, folio 152; p. 167, folio 163; p. 177, folio 173; p. 183,
  folio 179; pp. 185–210, folios 181–206). The only Pakistani places those chunks name — **ملتان** and
  **ٹھٹھ[OCR?]** — appear solely as stops on Shah Kamal's itinerary and carry no shrine claim (p. 209,
  folio 205).

- **pp. 211–303.** Six archive rows are engaged in pp. 211–303. They are ordered here by the weight of material.
  **One of them, `shrine-of-mian-mir`, is engaged by three separate bodies of material that the chunk
  workers placed differently; all three are kept, and the disagreement is stated in the entry and again
  under Doubts.**

### shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya — حضرت شیخ بہاء الدین زکریا, Hazrat Shaikh Baha al-Din Zakariya, as باب 7 heads him (p. 48, folio 44); with the nisba, "حضرت شیخ بہاء الدین زکریا ملتانی" (p. 74, folio 70; pp. 113–114, folios 109–110; p. 234, folio 230) and "حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا ملتانی" (p. 222, folio 218); without the nisba, "حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ" (p. 36, folio 32; p. 76, folio 72; p. 133, folio 129) and "حضرت شیخ بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ" (p. 135, folio 131); in his own printed verse the signature form "بہاء الدین ملتانی", Baha al-Din Multani (p. 53, folio 49); "بہاء الدین فرشتہ", Bahauddin Farishta, the name he became famous by at بخارا, Bukhara (p. 50, folio 46); "شیخ بہاء الدین غوث العالم", Shaikh Bahauddin Ghaus al-Alam, as the Prophet names him in the khirqa dream (p. 51, folio 47); "شیخ جلال" is another man, not him (p. 36, folio 32); kunya "ابو محمد", Abu Muhammad, with "بعض نے آپ کی کنیت ابو البرکات بھی لکھی ہے", some also writing **ابو البرکات**, Abu al-Barakat (p. 49, folio 45, حاشیہ 8)

- **Saint.** **The three ranges are grossly unequal and the reader must see it.** pp. 1–109 treats him at chapter length — باب 7 runs pp. 48–52 (folios 44–48) to its break, with the chapter's closing pages at pp. 53–55 (folios 49–51, **provisionally attributed, flagged under the pp. 1–109 input's Doubts D4**), plus his appearances as arbitrator in باب 2 (pp. 35–37, folios 31–33), as father and pir at the head of باب 11 (pp. 74–76, folios 70–72), and as a named contemporary in باب 8 (pp. 59, 63–64; folios 55, 59–60). pp. 110–210 makes him **never the subject of a chapter**: he is named throughout باب 17 (pp. 113–114, folios 109–110) and at three points in باب 22 (pp. 133, 135; folios 129, 131). pp. 211–303 engages him **twice, both times incidentally, and the two mentions do not overlap** (p. 222, folio 218; p. 234, folio 230).
  - **Identification and placement cautions, carried from all three inputs.** In pp. 110–210 the town agrees with the archive row by the book's own nisba, "**ملتانی**", used at every mention (pp. 113–114, folios 109–110), and the Suhrawardi affiliation is carried there **only** in the family khirqa of Shaikh Shihab al-Din Suhrawardi (p. 114, folio 110); باب 22 adds an independent placement — Jalal al-Din Surkh Bukhari "ملتان آئے۔ حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مرید ہوئے", came to **ملتان**, Multan, and became his murid there (p. 133, folio 129) — which the pp. 110–210 worker records as **that range's only sentence placing Baha al-Din Zakariya himself, and it places him at Multan** (p. 133, folio 129). In pp. 211–303 the nisba "**ملتانی**" is the only locational element attached to him at p. 234 (folio 230). **A second, distinct "بہاء الدین" occupies باب 41 of the same pp. 211–303 chunk (p. 214, folio 210) and must not be merged with him** (p. 214, folio 210).
  - **Titles as printed**: "پیر لاثانی", peerless pir; "مقبول بارگاہ رحمانی"; "برہان ملت"; "گنج عزلت"; "مخزن سخا", treasury of generosity; "معدن وفا"; "کان صفا"; "قطب الاولیاء"; "شیخ الاتقیاء"; "قدوۃ الاصفیاء"; and "اکابر اولیاء میں آپ کا شمار ہے", he is counted among the greatest of the auliya (p. 48, folio 44). In the khirqa dream the Prophet names him "شیخ بہاء الدین غوث العالم" (p. 51, folio 47).
  - **Silsila as the book gives it — a relationship, never an order name.** "آپ حضرت شیخ شہاب الدین عمر سہروردی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مرید اور خلیفہ ہیں" — murid and khalifa of **شیخ شہاب الدین عمر سہروردی**, Shaikh Shihab al-Din Umar Suhrawardi (p. 48, folio 44); the bai'at was taken at **بغداد**, Baghdad (p. 50, folio 46). **The book nowhere uses the word "سہروردیہ" as an order name in باب 7** (pp. 48–52, folios 44–48); chunk 004 records **silsila: (not stated)** for pp. 74–76 (folios 70–72); the pp. 110–210 range records **silsila: (not stated)** (pp. 113–114, 133, 135; folios 109–110, 129, 131); and the pp. 211–303 range records **silsila: (not stated)** in both mentions (p. 222, folio 218; p. 234, folio 230), which its worker labels "silence, not conflict". The relationship stated agrees with the archive row's "Suhrawardi" without the book naming the order (p. 48, folio 44).
  - **UNRECONCILED alongside the above — a Qadiri threshold in the book's own printed verse.** The same chapter prints his famous **منقبت, manqabat**, in honour of Ghaus al-Azam Shaikh Abd al-Qadir Jilani (p. 52, folio 48), whose signature couplet is "سنگ درگاہ جیلانی بہاء الدین ملتانی / لقائے[OCR?] دین سلطانی محی الدین جیلانی" — "Baha al-Din Multani is a stone of the threshold of Jilani" (p. 53, folio 49). **The Suhrawardi bai'at-and-khilafat statement (p. 48, folio 44) and this Qadiri-threshold verse (p. 53, folio 49) stand side by side; no input reconciles them and this merge does not either.**
  - **UNRECONCILED — lineage given as four accounts, and then, uniquely, adjudicated.** "آپ قریشی' اسدی' ہاشمی تھے" — Qurashi, Asadi, Hashimi (p. 48, folio 44, حاشیہ 1). "یہ بھی کہا جاتا ہے" that he was of the progeny of "مہیار[OCR?] بن اسود بن مطلب بن اسد بن عبد العزیٰ[OCR?] بن قصی" (p. 48, folio 44, حاشیہ 2). Some accepted only "القرشی الاسدی" (p. 48, folio 44, حاشیہ 3). Some wrote, in Persian, "سلسلہ او باسد قریش کہ جد مادری حضرت امیر المومنین علی کرم اللہ وجہہ منتہی می شود", his lineage ends at Asad of Quraish, the maternal grandfather of Ali, with the book printing its own Urdu translation beneath it (p. 48, folio 44, حاشیہ 4). **Then the one adjudication in the whole pp. 1–109 range**: "آپ ہاشمی ہیں اور آپ کے ہاشمی ہونے میں کوئی شک و شبہ نہیں" — he is Hashimi and in his being Hashimi there is no doubt whatever (p. 48, folio 44, حاشیہ 5).
  - **The family's route to Multan.** His "جد امجد" **Maulana Shaikh Kamal al-Din Aliya Shah[OCR?]**, a dervish-natured elder and honoured member of the Quraish, "مکہ سے سکونت ترک کرکے خوارزم میں رہے' پھر خوارزم سے ملتان تشریف لائے اور وہاں مستقل سکونت اختیار کی" — left **مکہ**, Mecca, lived in **خوارزم**, Khwarazm, then came from Khwarazm to **ملتان**, Multan, and settled there permanently (p. 48, folio 44, حاشیہ 6), where many became his devotees (p. 49, folio 45). **The book's stated reason**: "ملتان اس زمانہ میں اسلامی علوم و فنون کا مرکز تھا" — Multan in that age was a centre of Islamic sciences and arts (p. 48, folio 44).
  - **Father**: **شیخ وجیہہ الدین**, Shaikh Wajih al-Din (p. 49, folio 45).
  - **UNRECONCILED — two maternal grandfathers printed side by side, neither preferred.** The mother is "بی بی فاطمہ", Bibi Fatima (p. 49, folio 45). (a) She is given first as the daughter of **مولانا حسام الدین ترمذی**, Maulana Husam al-Din Tirmizi, who came from his homeland **ترمذ**, Tirmiz, to the fort of "قلعہ کوٹ کڑور[OCR?]" when the **Tatar plundering** began (p. 49, folio 45, حاشیہ 7). (b) Then, "یہ بھی کہا جاتا ہے", as the daughter of **حضرت عیسیٰ**, Hazrat Isa, of the progeny of Ghaus al-Azam Shaikh Jilani, resident at "ہامہ[OCR?]", who married her to Shaikh Wajih al-Din when he visited there (p. 49, folio 45). **Both accounts end with the family at Kot Karor[OCR?]; the book does not choose, and the pp. 1–109 input directs that any entry using his descent must give both** (p. 49, folio 45).
  - **Birth as printed**: "آپ صبح کے وقت' جمعہ کے دن 27 رمضان 566ھ کو کوٹ کڑور میں پیدا ہوئے" — born in the morning, on a **جمعہ**, Friday, **27 رمضان 566ھ**, 27 Ramazan 566 AH, at **کوٹ کڑور**, Kot Karor (p. 49, folio 45). Name: بہاء الدین, Bahauddin (p. 49, folio 45). Kunya **ابو محمد**, Abu Muhammad, and "بعض نے آپ کی کنیت ابو البرکات بھی لکھی ہے", some also wrote **ابو البرکات**, Abu al-Barakat — **an unreconciled pair, the book preferring neither** (p. 49, folio 45, حاشیہ 8). The pp. 110–210 range records **birth: (not stated)** (pp. 113–114, 133, 135; folios 109–110, 129, 131).
  - **Born a wali.** "آپ مادر زاد ولی تھے" (p. 49, folio 45) — as an infant he would stop suckling to listen when his father recited the Quran, and while still at maktab said "جس وقت خداوند تعالیٰ نے الست بربکم فرمایا تھا' اس وقت سے لے کر اب تک کے واقعات مجھے یاد ہیں", I remember what has happened from the moment God said *Am I not your Lord?* until now (p. 49, folio 45, حاشیہ 9). **His father died when he was twelve** (p. 49, folio 45).
  - **Training, quantified.** Memorised the Quran "سات سال کی عمر میں سات قرات کے ساتھ", at **seven** with the **seven readings**; after his father's death went to **خراسان**, Khurasan, and spent **seven years** in the outward lessons (p. 50, folio 46, حاشیہ 10); then **بخارا**, Bukhara, where he completed his learning and "وہاں آپ 'بہاء الدین فرشتہ' کے نام سے مشہور ہوئے", became famous there as **Bahauddin Farishta**; then **مدینہ**, Medina, where he took hadith from **شیخ کمال الدین محمد یمنی**, of the great muhaddithin, and "اجازت نامہ بھی حاصل کیا", obtained an **اجازت نامہ, ijazat-nama**, as well, staying **five years** (p. 50, folio 46). He met dervishes in "خراسان' بخارا' مکہ' مدینہ اور بیت المقدس" — Khurasan, Bukhara, Mecca, Medina and Bait al-Muqaddas (p. 50, folio 46).
  - **Seventy days that caused resentment, the numeral OCR-flagged.** Nizam al-Din Auliya is quoted that he stayed "ستر[OCR?] روز" in Shihab al-Din Umar Suhrawardi's service and received much bounty; the other murids resented it, and the shaykh answered "تم مثل لکڑی کے تر ہو اور زکریا مثل لکڑی خشک کے ہے' پس آگ خشک لکڑی کو جلد پکڑتی ہے" (p. 50, folio 46, حاشیہ 11).
  - **On the road to Multan** he met a learned qalandar, **سید عبد القدوس**, Sayyid Abd al-Quddus, of **موصل**, Mosul, who had donned the qalandars' dress at the grave of **سید جمال الدین مجرد**; "آپ نے ان کا قلندرانہ جامہ اتروایا اور عالم جذب سے ان کو عالم سلوک کو پہنچایا" — he had the qalandari garment removed and brought him from *jazb* to *suluk* (p. 51, folio 47). **شیخ جلال الدین تبریزی**, Shaikh Jalal al-Din Tabrizi, travelled with him as far as **نیشاپور**, Nishapur, where they parted (p. 51, folio 47).
  - **Arrival at Multan and the dervishes' objection.** "آپ نے ملتان پہنچ کر مستقل سکونت اختیار فرمائی" — he took **permanent residence at ملتان, Multan**; the dervishes of Multan were displeased and tried to have him leave, sending a cup filled with **milk** to say "اس شہر میں دوسرے کی گنجائش نہیں ہے", there is no room for another in this city; he placed a **flower** on the cup and sent it back, meaning his place in the city would be "جیسے کہ پھول دودھ پر ہے", as a flower is on milk; they were astonished and became his devotees (p. 51, folio 47). The pp. 1–109 worker notes that **the identical milk-and-flower idiom, and the identical claim of single-occupancy jurisdiction over a town, recur at Panipat between two other saints** (p. 94, folio 90).
  - **Death as printed, in one range only.** "آپ 7 صفر 666ھ کو بروز جمعرات جوار رحمت میں داخل ہوئے" — **7 صفر 666ھ**, 7 Safar 666 AH, on a **جمعرات**, Thursday (p. 52, folio 48, حاشیہ 15), "آپ کی عمر (100) سو سال کی تھی", his age **one hundred years** (p. 52, folio 48). The pp. 1–109 worker's own observation — **an observation of the chunk worker, not a statement of the book** — is that birth 566ھ and death 666ھ at age 100 are "internally consistent as printed" (pp. 49, 52; folios 45, 48); no interval is computed here. **No Common-Era year is printed anywhere in any range and none is computed here**; the pp. 1–109 input flags the archive row's `1267` under its Doubts D5 (p. 52, folio 48). **Silence elsewhere, not conflict**: chunk 004 records **death year: (not stated)** for pp. 74–76 (folios 70–72); pp. 110–210 records **death year: (not stated)** (pp. 113–114, 133, 135; folios 109–110, 129, 131); pp. 211–303 records **death year: (not stated)** in both mentions (p. 222, folio 218; p. 234, folio 230).
  - **Sons — the count is lost, the names are not.** "آپ کے ساتھ [OCR?] لڑکے تھے", he had [OCR?] sons — **the numeral is lost** — and seven names follow: **شیخ صدر الدین عارف، شیخ برہان الدین، شیخ ضیاء الدین، شیخ علاوٴ الدین [OCR?]، شیخ شہاب الدین، شیخ قدوۃ الدین، شیخ شمس الدین** (p. 52, folio 48). **The pp. 1–109 input directs: cite the names, not a count** (p. 52, folio 48).
  - **Household habits.** He completed one Quran every night; "تحمل و بردباری' ذوق و شوق' بے ہوشی اور استغراق آپ کی نمایاں خصوصیات تھیں"; and "آپ کو سماع کا شوق تھا", **he was fond of سماع, sama** (p. 52, folio 48). He fasted much in early life but "آخری عمر میں روزانہ روزہ نہ رکھتے تھے", did not fast daily in his last years (p. 52, folio 48, حاشیہ 17).
  - **Poet.** "آپ شاعر بھی تھے", and he has a famous **منقبت, manqabat**, in honour of Ghaus al-Azam Shaikh Abd al-Qadir Jilani, "جس کے چند اشعار ذیل میں پیش کئے" — a few verses of which are presented below (p. 52, folio 48). **The chapter breaks off at that point** and the verses fall on the following page (p. 52, folio 48).
  - **The verses, on the next page** (provisionally the same chapter — the pp. 1–109 input's Doubts D4): six couplets with the refrain **"محی الدین جیلانی"** — "معلا[OCR?] جب سبحانی مقدس قطب ربانی …", "مدد یا شاہ جیلانی بریں افتادہ حیرانی …" — closing on the signature couplet **"سنگ درگاہ جیلانی بہاء الدین ملتانی / لقائے[OCR?] دین سلطانی محی الدین جیلانی"** (p. 53, folio 49).
  - **Family relations named in pp. 110–210.** He is Rukn al-Din's **grandfather (دادا)**, Bibi Rasti's **pir** and **father-in-law (خسر)** (p. 113, folio 109), and he supervised Rukn al-Din's "تعلیم و تربیت", education and upbringing (p. 114, folio 110).
  - **In pp. 211–303 he appears as the occupant of a visited mazar and as an ancestor.** "ملتان میں آپ حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا ملتانی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار پر انوار پر حاضر ہوئے اور شیخ صدر الدین سے ملاقات کی" — in **ملتان**, Multan, Shaikh Jamali presented himself at the luminous mazar of Hazrat Baha al-Din Zakariya Multani and met **شیخ صدر الدین**, Shaikh Sadr al-Din (p. 222, folio 218); the nisba as printed there is "ملتانی" and the town as printed is "ملتان" (p. 222, folio 218). Of Shaikh Hamza Dharsawi (باب 47): "آپ حضرت شیخ بہاء الدین زکریا ملتانی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کی اولاد ہیں" — he is of the descendants of Hazrat Shaikh Baha al-Din Zakariya Multani (p. 234, folio 230). **Caution: the inputs print "شیخ صدر الدین" at p. 222 (folio 218) with no further identification, and print "حضرت شیخ صدر الدین عارف" as son, khalifa and sajjada nashin at p. 52 (folio 48) and p. 74 (folio 70); no input joins the two, and this merge does not join them.**

- **Shrine / Darbar.** Across all three ranges the book yields two sentences that put his mazar at Multan, one that names the mazar with no town, and nothing else.
  - **His mazar is in Multan and is visited (pp. 1–109).** Maulana Hussam al-Din relates that "جب وہ ملتان میں تھے تو ایک دن وہ حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار سے واپس ہو رہے تھے" — when he was in **ملتان**, Multan, he was one day **returning from the mazar** of Baha al-Din Zakariya (p. 76, folio 72). The pp. 1–109 worker records this as **the only locational statement about the shrine in that whole range, and incidental** — it occurs in another saint's chapter, as the setting of a karamat (p. 76, folio 72).
  - **His mazar is in Multan and is visited (pp. 211–303).** "ملتان میں آپ حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا ملتانی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار پر انوار پر حاضر ہوئے" — in Multan the travelling shaykh presented himself at the **مزار پر انوار**, luminous mazar (p. 222, folio 218). **The mazar is described there only by the formula "مزار پر انوار"** (p. 222, folio 218).
  - **A mazar with no town beside it (pp. 110–210).** باب 22 records **his mazar as a site of Eid-night vigil**: "ایک سال عید کی رات کو آپ حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار مبارک پر یاد الٰہی میں مشغول رہے" (p. 133, folio 129). **Placement caution: the town of the mazar is (not stated) in that sentence** (p. 133, folio 129).
  - **باب 7 states nothing whatever about his own mazar, and that silence must not be read as the book's.** The chapter breaks off at p. 52 (folio 48) inside "شعر و شاعری"; **no statement about the site, the urs, the building or the endowment of his shrine occurs in pp. 48–52** (folios 44–48), and chunk 002 warns explicitly that **the archive must not take that silence as the book's silence** — the rest of the chapter is at pp. 53–55 (folios 49–51), and that fragment likewise "states no death year, no shrine, no urs, no custodianship and no silsila" (pp. 48–55, folios 44–51).
  - **No shrine statement, no location for his own grave, no building date, no urs and no custodianship appear for him in باب 17** — "(not stated)" (pp. 113–114, folios 109–110).
  - **Nothing on buildings, urs, custodianship, endowment or a building date in pp. 211–303** (p. 222, folio 218).
  - **Urs, custodianship of his own darbar, endowment, buildings, Auqaf, visitor numbers, disputes: (not stated)** anywhere in pp. 1–109 (pp. 48–55, 74–76; folios 44–51, 70–72). The one succession statement that range carries is about the person of the successor, not about the shrine (p. 52, folio 48, حاشیہ 16) — see Practices & Events.

- **Practices & Events.**
  - **The only custodianship statement, and it is hereditary and distinguishes two kinds of succession.** "آپ کے لڑکے حضرت شیخ صدر الدین عارف آپ کے خلیفہ اور سجادہ نشین تھے" — his son **حضرت شیخ صدر الدین عارف**, Hazrat Shaikh Sadr al-Din Arif, was his **خلیفہ** and **سجادہ نشین**, khalifa and sajjada nashin — set against a separate list of five "مقتدر خلفاء", eminent khalifas, who are not sons (p. 52, folio 48, حاشیہ 16). **Succession to the seat and succession to the teaching are distinguished on one page** (p. 52, folio 48). The same relation is restated at the head of باب 11: "حضرت شیخ صدر الدین عارف اپنے والد ماجد حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا ملتانی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے فرزند' مرید' خلیفہ اور **جانشین** ہیں" — son, murid, khalifa and *janishin*, successor (p. 74, folio 70), and "اپنے والد … سے بیعت ہوئے اور انہیں سے خرقہ خلافت پایا" — took bai'at at his father's hand and received the **خرقہ خلافت**, khirqa of khilafat, from him (p. 74, folio 70).
  - **The five eminent khalifas who are not sons**: **حضرت سید جلال الدین سرخ بخاری**, **حضرت سید عثمان معروف بہ لعل شہباز سندی[OCR?]**, **حضرت شیخ حسن افغان**, **حضرت شیخ فخر الدین ابراہیم عراقی**, **حضرت سید صدر الدین احمد معروف بہ سید حسین** (p. 52, folio 48, حاشیہ 16). **The book gives none of them a place, a date or a shrine** (p. 52, folio 48). The pp. 1–109 input records that two of the five collide with archive rows, under its Doubts D6 and D7 (p. 52, folio 48).
  - **He conducts the succession in person (pp. 110–210).** The turban episode at p. 114 (folio 110) is his act and his words: "بابا صدر الدین! تم اس کو منع نہ کرو…" (p. 114, folio 110).
  - **A langar open to all, on the back of large-scale agriculture and trade.** "آپ کے یہاں زراعت اور تجارت بڑے پیمانے پر ہوتی تھی' ہر حال میں خدا کا شکر ادا کرتے تھے' آپ کا لنگر عام تھا' آپ بہت مخیر تھے' دنیا سے بے پروا تھے' سادگی اور قناعت کے ساتھ زندگی گزارتے تھے' بہت کم کھاتے تھے' لیکن قوی غذا کھاتے تھے' علماء کرام مشائخ عظام اور مہمانوں کی بہت عزت اور خاطر کرتے تھے" — at his place **agriculture and trade were carried on on a large scale**; he gave thanks in every condition; **his لنگر, langar, was عام, general**, open to all; he was very charitable, unconcerned with the world, lived with simplicity and contentment, ate very little but strong food, and greatly honoured the ulama, the great mashaikh and guests (p. 52, folio 48, حاشیہ 17). The pp. 1–109 worker reads this as **the clearest statement in that range of a shrine household's productive base: the langar funded by farming and commerce, not by a grant** (p. 52, folio 48).
  - **UNRECONCILED — the estate at his death, and an identical sum attached to a different event.** (a) "والد کی وفات کے بعد (70) ستر لاکھ ٹنکہ [OCR?] آپ کے حصہ میں آیا جو آپ نے فقراء کو تقسیم کر دیا" — after his father's death **ستر لاکھ ٹنکہ[OCR?]**, seventy lakh tanka[OCR?], came to Sadr al-Din Arif's share, which he distributed to the fuqara (p. 74, folio 70). (b) **An identical figure, "ستر لاکھ ٹکہ[OCR?]", seventy lakh tanka[OCR?] — note the different spelling of the unit, both OCR-flagged — is the value of the Multan distribution of the maritime offering at p. 54 (folio 50)**. **The pp. 1–109 input flags the duplication under its Doubts D8; the two statements are attached to different events and neither input reconciles them, nor does this merge** (p. 74, folio 70; p. 54, folio 50). The heir's reasoning at (a) is printed in full: "میرے والد دنیا پر غالب تھے' اس لئے مال و زر کی موجودگی ان کو کوئی نقصان نہ پہنچا سکتی تھی … اگرچہ میں بفضلہ تعالیٰ دنیا پر بیشتر غالب ہوں' مگر کبھی کبھی درجہ مساویت بھی ہو جاتا ہے' اس واسطے میں اس اندیشے سے کہ مبادا کبھی دنیا مجھ پر غالب آ جائے مطمئن نہیں ہوں' اس واسطے میں نے اسباب دنیاوی کو اپنے پاس سے دور پھینک دیا" (pp. 74–75, folios 70–71, حاشیہ 2 = سالک السالکین جلد دوم ص 526). The pp. 1–109 worker records (a) as **the largest sum in that range attached to this shrine's founding family, and the only statement about its inherited wealth** (p. 74, folio 70).
  - **A maritime offering distributed in full within thirty days.** After the ship reached **عدن**, Aden, safely, "مسافروں نے اپنا تہائی مال خواجہ کمال الدین کو **بطور نذرانہ و شکرانہ** دیا" — the passengers gave **a third of their goods** to Khwaja Kamal al-Din as **نذرانہ و شکرانہ**, nazrana and shukrana; he sent all of it, with **half his own jewels**, to Multan by his nephew; "وہ مال و جواہرات آپ نے **تیس دن کے اندر** لوگوں کو تقسیم کر دیا' اس مال کی قیمت **ستر لاکھ ٹکہ[OCR?]** تھی" — the saint distributed the whole to the people **within thirty days**, the value of the goods being **seventy lakh tanka[OCR?]** (p. 54, folio 50, note 19 → سالک السالکین جلد دوم ص 521،522). The pp. 1–109 worker reads this as **offerings arriving from Indian Ocean trade rather than from the land, with a stated disbursement timetable** (p. 54, folio 50).
  - **A grave plot asked for beside the shrine, and the successor's answer on jurisdiction.** Maulana Hussam al-Din, in Multan, returning from Baha al-Din Zakariya's mazar, conceived the wish "کہ وہ اپنی قبر کے واسطے ایک قطعہ زمین مانگیں" — to ask for a **plot of ground for his own grave**; Sadr al-Din Arif knew it by kashf and said "مجھے ایک قبر کی جگہ آپ کو دینی مشکل نہیں' اور نہ عذر ہے' لیکن آپ کے واسطے حضرت سرور عالم صلی اللہ علیہ وسلم نے ایک جگہ بدایوں میں تجویز فرمائی ہے' آپ کا مدفن اس جگہ ہوگا" — it is not difficult for me to give you a grave-place, nor do I object, but for you the Prophet has designated a place at **بدایوں**, Badaun; your burial will be there (p. 76, folio 72). The pp. 1–109 worker reads this as **the clearest statement in that range of a sajjada's disposal of burial ground inside a shrine precinct, framed as a power he holds and declines to use** (p. 76, folio 72). **Attribution of the story to a source is uncertain, flagged under that input's Doubts D19** (p. 76, folio 72).
  - **A state religious office held, as the outcome of a slander case.** "سلطان شمس الدین التمش نے نجم الدین صغریٰ[OCR?] کو شیخ الاسلام کے عہدے سے علیحدہ کر دیا … اس جھوٹے بہتان کی سزا میں نجم الدین صغریٰ کو اپنے عہدے سے دست بردار ہونا پڑا۔ نجم الدین صغریٰ کے بجائے آپ شیخ الاسلام مقرر ہوئے" — Sultan Shams al-Din Altamsh removed **نجم الدین صغریٰ[OCR?]** from the office of **شیخ الاسلام**, Shaikh al-Islam; as punishment for the false slander he had to relinquish it, and **in his place Bahauddin Zakariya was appointed Shaikh al-Islam** (pp. 51–52, folios 47–48, حاشیہ 14). The pp. 1–109 worker records this as **the most direct statement of state office held by a figure the archive carries**; the entry gives it its own headed section, "عہدہ" (pp. 51–52, folios 47–48).
  - **He acts twice as an authority in باب 2, not as a subject.** He rebukes Jalal al-Din Tabrizi for not naming Shihab al-Din Suhrawardi before Farid al-Din Attar, is displeased and separates from him at Nishapur (p. 35, folio 31, حاشیہ 6); and at **دہلی**, Delhi, chosen as **ثالث**, arbitrator, of a shariah tribunal, he lifts the accused saint's shoes in his own hands and declares "میرے اوپر واجب ہے کہ شیخ جلال کی خاک پا کو اپنی آنکھوں کا سرمہ بناؤں' کیوں کہ وہ سات برس تک سفر و خضر[OCR?] میں میرے پیر و مرشد کی خدمت میں مقیم رہے ہیں" — it is obligatory on me to make the dust of Shaikh Jalal's feet the kohl of my eyes, because for **seven years** in travel and residence[OCR?] he stayed in the service of my pir and murshid — and then examines the woman, who confesses the whole plot (pp. 36–37, folios 32–33, حاشیہ 9). **The accuser had counted on the earlier estrangement**: "ان کو خیال تھا کہ حضرت بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کی آپ سے نیشاپور میں کشیدگی ہو گئی تھی" (p. 36, folio 32).
  - **The tribunal itself, in which he is the pivot**: "جب سلطان التمش کے پاس شکایت پہنچی تو اس نے تمام مشاہیر اور مشائخ کو دہلی بلایا۔ دو سو سے زیادہ اولیائے کرام دہلی آئے۔ جمعہ کی نماز کے بعد سب مسجد منار میں جمع ہوئے" — **more than two hundred** auliya came to Delhi; after the Friday prayer all assembled in the **مسجد منار**, Masjid Minar; the Sultan let the accuser name the arbitrator; when the accused entered "سب آپ کی تعظیم کے لئے کھڑے ہو گئے", all stood in respect, and the Sultan said "جب ثالث نے اتنی عزت کی تو پھر معاملہ طے ہو گیا", when the arbitrator has shown such honour the matter is decided (p. 36, folio 32, حاشیہ 9).
  - **He is quoted assessing another saint**: "اگر احمد کی مشغولی وزن کی جائے تو دس صوفیوں کی مشغولی کے برابر ہو گی", if Ahmad's absorption were weighed it would equal that of ten Sufis (p. 46, folio 42).
  - **He corresponds with Baba Farid.** He writes "میان ما و شما عشق بازی است", between us and you is love-play, to which Farid replies "میان ما و شما عشق ہست و بازی نیست", between us and you is love, and no play (pp. 63–64, folios 59–60, note 20). He is also among the mashaikh whose company Farid benefited from (p. 59, folio 55, note 13).
  - **He confers khilafat on Jalal al-Din Surkh Bukhari** the same day as the hail karamat: "آپ نے ان کو اسی روز خرقہ خلافت سے مشرف فرمایا" (p. 54, folio 50).
  - **He founds the Uch line from Multan by an order of residence (pp. 110–210).** Jalal al-Din Surkh Bukhari became his murid at Multan, "ان ہی سے خرقہ خلافت پایا' اپنے پیرو مرشد کے حکم سے اوچہ میں سکونت اختیار کی" — received the **خرقہ خلافت**, khirqa of khilafat, from him and **took up residence at اوچہ, Uch, by his pir's order** (p. 133, folio 129). The pp. 110–210 worker reads the settlement at Uch as **presented as an instruction issued from Multan, not a choice** (p. 133, folio 129).
  - **His khirqa is a heritable object crossing two generations of another family at another shrine.** Makhdum Jahaniyan "حضرت شیخ بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کا خرقہ اپنے والد سید احمد کبیر سے پہنا" — wore Baha al-Din Zakariya's khirqa, received from his own father **سید احمد کبیر**, Sayyid Ahmad Kabir (p. 135, folio 131).
  - **He names the child and predicts him before birth**, rising for his pregnant daughter-in-law: "یہ تعظیم اس بچے کی ہے۔ جو تیرے پیٹ میں ہے۔ وہ چراغ خاندان و شمع دو دمان کا ہو گا" (p. 113, folio 109, footnote 1 → "سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 527").
  - **Two teachings on wealth**, from the fragment at p. 53 (note 18 → سالک السالکین جلد دوم ص 515): "مال دنیا کتنا ہی کیوں نہ ہو' تاہم قلیل ہے اور سانپ کی صحبت اس شخص کو نقصان پہنچاتی ہے' جو افسوں اس کا نہ جانتا ہو' میرے نزدیک مال دنیا کی کوئی حقیقت نہیں ہے" — however much worldly wealth there is, it is still little, and the company of a snake harms one who does not know its charm; in my view worldly wealth has no reality; and "فقیروں کے نزدیک عدم اور وجود مال دنیا کا یکساں ہے' نہ جانے کا غم' نہ آنے کی خوشی" — for faqirs the absence and presence of worldly wealth are the same, no grief at its going, no joy at its coming (p. 53, folio 49).
  - **Aqwal zarin** (same fragment): "زہد تین چیزیں ہیں' جس میں یہ نہیں وہ زاہد نہیں" — zuhd is three things and whoever lacks them is no ascetic: knowing the truth of the world and letting go of it; serving the Lord and keeping to his adab; longing for and seeking the hereafter. And: safety lies in little food, the soul's safety in leaving people, and the faith's safety in the prayer of the Best of Creation (p. 53, folio 49).
  - **A mosque with a khadim, prayer rows and a courtyard** used as the stage of the hail karamat: he "نے اپنے ایک خادم کو مسجد میں بھیجا اور یہ تاکید فرمائی صفیں لپیٹ کر مسجد کے صحن میں جھاڑو دیں" — sent a **خادم**, khadim, with orders to roll up the prayer rows and sweep the courtyard, and himself came at **ظہر**, Zuhr (pp. 53–54, folios 49–50).
  - **Multan stands as one stop on an itinerary of shrine visitation (pp. 211–303)**, between the Haramain and مصر (Egypt), بغداد (Baghdad), بیت المقدس, روم, شام, عراق, عرب و عجم, آزربائجان, گیلان, مازندران and خراسان (p. 222, folio 218, حاشیہ 3 = المشاہیر صفحہ 209). At the same stop a **living successor** is met, شیخ صدر الدین (p. 222, folio 218).

- **Legends & Miracles.**
  - **The khirqa dream and its waking confirmation.** He saw in a dream a house of light with the Prophet present, his pir standing respectfully, and many khirqas hanging on a rope; he was summoned, his hand taken, and the Prophet said "اے عمر! اس خرقہ کو شیخ بہاء الدین غوث العالم کو پہنا دو", O Umar, clothe Shaikh Bahauddin Ghaus al-Alam in this khirqa; in the morning the pir called him, "آپ نے مکان اسی طرح کا پایا' جیسا کہ خواب میں دیکھا تھا", he found the house exactly as in the dream, and the same khirqa was taken down "طنطاب [OCR?] سے اتار کر" and put on him by hand, the pir disclaiming any authority of his own: "یہ سب خرقے سرور عالم ﷺ کے ہیں' میں تو درمیان میں صرف ایک واسطہ ہوں' بے اجازت سرور عالم ﷺ کے کسی کو نہیں پہنا سکتا" (pp. 50–51, folios 46–47, حواشی 12–13). After conferring it the pir "آپ کو ملتان جانے کی تاکید فرمائی", enjoined him to go to Multan (p. 51, folio 47) — **the book's stated reason for his presence at Multan** (p. 51, folio 47).
  - **Memory of the primordial covenant**, claimed as a schoolboy: he remembered events from the moment God said "الست بربکم" (p. 49, folio 45, حاشیہ 9).
  - **The flower on the milk** (p. 51, folio 47), told as a wordless exchange rather than a marvel, ending with the dervishes of Multan becoming his devotees (p. 51, folio 47).
  - **Hail over a mosque in Multan and nowhere outside it.** Under کشف و کرامات: when Jalal al-Din Surkh Bukhari came to Multan and pined for Bukhara's weather, "آپ کو یہ بات کشف سے معلوم ہو گئی" — the saint knew it by **کشف, kashf**; he had the mosque swept, a cloud appeared, heavy rain fell and hailstones, "مسجد سے باہر نہ بارش ہوئی اور نہ اولے", and outside the mosque there was neither rain nor hail; at Zuhr he came smiling and asked "کہئے سید! اولے ملتان کے بہتر ہیں یا برف بخارا کا", to which the Sayyid answered "اس صورت میں تو اولے ملتان کے سو درجہ بہتر ہیں" (pp. 53–54, folios 49–50).
  - **A pir appearing on a storm-bound ship.** Khwaja Kamal al-Din Masud Shirazi called on him in the storm; "آپ جہاز پر نمودار ہوئے' سب مسافروں نے آپ کو بخوبی دیکھا", the saint appeared on the ship and all the passengers saw him plainly, gave tidings of safety, and the ship reached **عدن**, Aden, well; the nephew who carried the goods to Multan recognised him at once "اور اسی لباس میں پایا' جس میں انہوں نے آپ کو جہاز پر دیکھا تھا", in the very dress in which he had seen him on the ship (p. 54, folio 50, note 19).

- **Significance.**
  - **The book's interest in him is institutional as much as mystical**: باب 7 devotes a whole headed section, **"عہدہ"**, to the office of Shaikh al-Islam (pp. 51–52, folios 47–48) and another, **"خلفاء"**, to succession (p. 52, folio 48).
  - **Descent is the one thing the author treats as settled**: after printing four accounts of the lineage he writes "آپ ہاشمی ہیں اور آپ کے ہاشمی ہونے میں کوئی شک و شبہ نہیں" (p. 48, folio 44, حاشیہ 5). Nowhere else in pp. 1–109 does he adjudicate (pp. 48–55, 74–76; folios 44–51, 70–72).
  - **Agreement with the archive row, stated as agreement and not as new evidence**: the row's location (Multan) agrees with "ملتان پہنچ کر مستقل سکونت اختیار فرمائی" (p. 51, folio 47) and with the mazar statement (p. 76, folio 72); the row's silsila (Suhrawardi) agrees with "مرید اور خلیفہ" of Shihab al-Din Umar Suhrawardi (p. 48, folio 44). **No conflict on either field is recorded anywhere in pp. 1–109** (pp. 48–55, 74–76; folios 44–51, 70–72). The row's `figure_died` **1267** is neither confirmed nor contradicted (p. 52, folio 48, the pp. 1–109 input's Doubts D5).
  - **Within pp. 110–210 he functions as the origin-point of two archive rows at once**: the Multan sajjada transmitted to his grandson (p. 114, folio 110) and the Uch settlement ordered for his khalifa (p. 133, folio 129).
  - **Within pp. 211–303 the mazar's function in the book is to be a station on a journey, not a subject** — that range's worker records it as "a tazkirah-level record of shrine tourism as a stage in a shaykh's formation" (p. 222, folio 218).
  - **A second, distinct "بہاء الدین" occupies باب 41 of the pp. 211–303 chunk (p. 214, folio 210) and must not be merged with him** (p. 214, folio 210).

### shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar — حضرت بابا فرید الدین مسعود گنج شکر (باب 8, pp. 56–68, folios 52–64)

**Mapping status, stated plainly at the head because the three chunk workers disagree.** Chunk 003 maps
this figure to the archive row, on the strength of two explicit statements in باب 8 — "اجودھن میں جو آج کل
پاک پٹن کے نام سے مشہور ہے" (p. 59, folio 55) and "مزار پر انوار پاک پٹن میں فیوض و برکات کا سرچشمہ ہے"
(p. 62, folio 58) — and calls the identification "not in doubt". **Chunk 002 and chunk 004 each
deliberately declined to map the same name**, because their pages place him only at **کہتوال[OCR?]**
(p. 35, folio 31) and at **اجودھن** (pp. 78, 79, 82, 92; folios 74, 75, 78, 88) and give no mazar, no
death year and no silsila by name. **The disagreement is unreconciled and is recorded at Doubts D1.** The
material from all three chunks is gathered here, with each bullet marked to the pages it comes from, so
that a human can move the unmapped portion out again without losing it.

**Saint** *(from باب 8 unless marked)*

- **Titles.** "برہان الشریعت ہیں' سلطان الطریقت ہیں' گنج حقیقت ہیں" — proof of the shari'a, sultan of the
  tariqa, treasure of reality (p. 56, folio 52). **Note: the identical three-title formula opens باب 1 for
  Ali Hujwiri** at p. 21 (folio 17) — a formula of the book's register, not a distinguishing fact.
- **Lineage and family.** His paternal genealogy reaches "امیر المومنین حضرت عمر بن خطاب رضی اللہ عنہ"
  (note 1); he was of the family of **فرخ شاہ**, king of **کابل**, Kabul; his highest ancestor was
