  martyred in the battle of Kabul; his grandfather **حضرت قاضی شعیب فاروق** came with three sons and his
  goods to **لاہور**, Lahore, went from Lahore to **قصور**, Kasur, was appointed **قاضی** of **کہتوال**,
  Kahtwal, and settled there (p. 56, folio 52).
- **Father**: **شیخ جمال الدین سلیمان** (p. 56, folio 52). **Mother**: **بی بی قرسم خاتون**, daughter of
  **مولانا وجیہہ الدین نجندی[OCR?]** (note 2). Elder brother **اعزا الدین[OCR?] محمود**; younger brother
  **حضرت نجیب الدین متوکل**, "آپ کے مرید اور خلیفہ تھے", his murid and khalifa; sister **ہاجرہ**, famous
  by the title **جمیلہ خاتون** (p. 56, folio 52). *(Note: the same mother's name and title, "ہاجرہ" known
  as "جمیلہ خاتون", is given in باب 12 as the mother of Ala al-Din Ahmad Sabir at p. 77, folio 73, where
  Sabir is also called Farid's sister's son — see Cross-cutting material.)*
- **Birth, two years printed side by side.** "آپ نے 575ھ میں اس عالم کو زینت بخشی" — he adorned this world
  in **575 AH** (note 3); "بعض نے آپ کی پیدائش 569ھ میں ہونا لکھا ہے" — some have written his birth as
  **569 AH** (note 4) (p. 56, folio 52). **The book does not choose; see Doubts D9 on the book's own
  arithmetic.**
- **Name and titles.** His name is **"مسعود"** (p. 57, folio 53). **حضرت فرید الدین عطار** bestowed the
  name Farid al-Din on him; "یہ بھی کہا جاتا ہے کہ فرید الدین لقب ہے' جو بارگاہ ایزدی سے آپ کو عطا ہوا" —
  it is also said that Farid al-Din is a title granted from the divine court (note 5) (p. 57, folio 53).
  He is famous by the title **"گنج شکر"**, for which the chapter gives **two independent reasons** — see
  Legends.
- **Bereavement and education.** His father died before he came to his senses (p. 58, folio 54). Early
  education at home; **memorised the Quran at twelve**; at **fifteen** he came to **ملتان**, Multan, and
  read the famous fiqh book **"نافع"** with **مولانا منہاج الدین ترمذی** (note 8), acquiring the religious
  sciences; then went to **قندھار**, Qandahar, and stayed **five years**, attaining high competence in
  tafsir, hadith, fiqh, sarf, nahw and logic (p. 58, folio 54).
- **Bai'at, in a teaching mosque at Multan.** He was sitting in a mosque in **"محلّہ سرائے حلوائی"**, where
  Minhaj al-Din Tirmizi used to give dars, studying the *Nafi*, when **حضرت خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کاکی**
  came to Multan and entered that same mosque; after prayer he came to him and was asked **"مسعود! تو چو
  می خوانی"**, Masud, what do you read?; the answer, "کتاب نافع"; Qutb Sahib: **"میدانی کہ نفع تو از ین
  نافع خواہد بود"**, do you know that your benefit will be from this *Nafi*?; he replied "مجھے تو حضرت کی
  قدم بوسی سے نفع ہو گا" and laid his head on Qutb Sahib's feet (note 9) (p. 58, folio 54). Qutb Sahib then
  left for Delhi and refused him permission to follow, ordering him to continue his studies; he accompanied
  him "تیس[OCR?] منزل تک", up to thirty[OCR?] stages (note 10) (pp. 58–59, folios 54–55).
- **Bai'at date and age.** "آپ 590ھ میں حضرت خواجہ قطب الدین بختیار کاکی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کی بیعت سے مشرف
  ہوئے' اس وقت آپ کی عمر پندرہ سال کی تھی" — bai'at in **590 AH**, aged **fifteen** (note 11) (p. 59,
  folio 55).
- **Silsila.** **The chapter names no order by name.** The transmission is called **"پیران چشت"** and
  **"خواجگان چشت"** (pp. 59, 61; folios 55, 57), which chunk 003 records as consistent with the archive
  row's Chishti without the book ever using the word. Chunk 004 records **silsila: (not stated)** for its
  pages (pp. 78–92, folios 74–88).
- **Delhi.** Having finished his studies he came to **دہلی in 595 AH** and lived in a **حجرہ** near the
  **غزنی کے دروازے**, the Ghazni gate (p. 59, folio 55).
- **Hansi.** From Delhi he went to **ہانسی** and occupied himself with worship and austerity (p. 59,
  folio 55). *(From باب 13: "حضرت بابا صاحب آپ سے بہت محبت کرتے تھے۔ آپ کی خاطر بارہ سال ہانسی میں رہے" —
  twelve years at Hansi; **the subject of the verb is unstated** (p. 84, folio 80, حاشیہ 2 = سیر الاولیاء
  فارسی ص 178) — see Doubts D10.)*
- **Travels and companions.** "آپ نے عراق' شام' سیوستان' غزنی' بخارا' قندھار و غرہ[OCR?] کی سیر و سیاحت
  فرمائی" — **عراق** Iraq, **شام** Syria, **سیوستان** Siwistan, **غزنی** Ghazni, **بخارا** Bukhara,
  **قندھار** Qandahar and **غرہ[OCR?]** — and benefited from the company of **شیخ شہاب الدین عمر سہروردی،
  شیخ سیف الدین خضری[OCR?]، شیخ سعید الدین جموی[OCR?]، حضرت شیخ بہاء الدین زکریا، حضرت اوحد الدین کرمانی**
  and **شیخ فرید الدین محمد عطار نیشا پوری** (note 13); also from **شیخ الاسلام اجل شیرازی** and **شیخ
  شہاب الدین زند و بس[OCR?]** (p. 59, folio 55). **Siwistan appears only as an itinerary point, attached
  to no shrine.**
- **Austerities.** In his own words: "میں بیس برس عالم تفکر میں کھڑا رہا' بالکل نہیں بیٹھا' میرے پاؤں سوج
  گئے تھے اور خون ان سے بہتا تھا" — **twenty years** standing in contemplation, never sitting, feet swollen
  and bleeding, and he does not remember whether he ate anything in those twenty years (note 14 → راحت
  القلوب اردو ترجمہ ص 300) (p. 60, folio 56). He once kept a **روزۂ طے**, once did a **چلہ معکوس**, an
  inverted chilla, once kept a **روزۂ داؤدی** (p. 60, folio 56).
- **Marriages and children.** Four marriages: **بی بی نہرزہ[OCR?]**, daughter of **سلطان غیاث الدین
  بلبن**; **بی بی کلثوم**, mother of شیخ نصر اللہ; **بی بی شارو[OCR?]**; **بی بی سکرے[OCR?]**. Five sons
  and three daughters; sons **خواجہ نصیر الدین نصر اللہ، شیخ شہاب الدین، شیخ بدر الدین سلیمان، شیخ نظام
  الدین، شیخ یعقوب**, and a further son **شیخ عبد اللہ** who died "کا مصغر سنی[OCR?] میں"; daughters
  **بی بی مستورہ، بی بی شریفہ، بی بی فاطمہ** (note 17) (pp. 61–62, folios 57–58). **The count does not add
  up — see Doubts D11.**
- **Death, two years printed side by side.** "آپ یاحی یا قیوم کہتے ہوئے 5 محرم الحرام 670ھ کو جوار رحمت
  میں داخل ہوئے" — saying *Ya Hayy, Ya Qayyum*, he entered the neighbourhood of mercy on **5 Muharram
  al-Haram 670 AH**; "بعض نے آپ کا سنہ وفات 664ھ لکھا ہے" — some have written his death year as **664 AH**
  (note 18, citing سیر الاولیاء ص 91، اخبار الاخیار ص 111 and سفینۃ الاولیاء ص 97) (p. 62, folio 58).
  **No CE date is given.** Against the archive row's **1266** — see Doubts D12.
- **Character and means.** Unrivalled ("بے نظر[OCR?]") in austerity and poverty, no lover of fame, much
  absorbed; "آپ کو سماع کا بہت شوق تھا", **he was very fond of sama'** (pp. 62–63, folios 58–59). Utterly
  obedient to his pir; "آپ کے گھر میں فقر و فاقہ رہتا تھا", poverty and hunger stayed in his house (p. 63,
  folio 59). His clothing was tattered; he had one blanket so small that covering his feet uncovered his
  head (p. 63, folio 59). He was also a scholar, unmatched in eloquence.
- **Writings.** In his famous book **"فوائد السالکین"** he collected his pir's malfuzat; the risalas
  **"موجود وجود"**, **"گفتار"** and **"الٰہی نامہ"** are "بھی آپ کی تصانیف بتائی جاتی ہیں", also *said to
  be* his compositions; "آپ شاعر بھی تھے", he was also a poet (p. 63, folio 59).

**Shrine / Darbar**

- **The shrine town, and the book recording the name change itself.** From Hansi he moved to **"اجودھن میں
  جو آج کل پاک پٹن کے نام سے مشہور ہے"** — Ajodhan, **which nowadays is famous by the name of Pak Pattan**
  (p. 59, folio 55). **The book writes the modern name as two words, "پاک پٹن".**
- **The mazar.** "مزار پر انوار پاک پٹن میں فیوض و برکات کا سرچشمہ ہے" — his radiant mazar **at Pak Pattan**
  is a fountainhead of graces and blessings (p. 62, folio 58). **This is the statement on which the
  archive mapping rests.**
- **The physical fabric of the grave.** "جب آپ کا وصال ہوا تو گھر میں کفن کے لئے پیسہ نہ تھا۔ مکان کا
  دروازہ توڑ کر اس کی اینٹیں قبر میں لگائی گئیں" — when he died there was no money in the house for a
  shroud; **the door of the house was broken up and its bricks were set in the grave** (note 19) (p. 63,
  folio 59). **A statement about the material of the grave at Pak Pattan and about the estate the shrine
  began from.**
- **No urs date, no urs at all, no custodianship arrangement for the shrine, no endowment, no Auqaf, no
  visitor numbers, no building history and no dispute over the shrine is given anywhere: (not stated)**
  (pp. 56–68, folios 52–64; and chunk 004's pages, pp. 78–92, folios 74–88).
- **An opponent in office at the shrine town.** "قاضی اجودھن آپ کا سخت مخالف تھا' لیکن اس کی مخالفت کارگر
  نہ ہوئی' آپ برابر رشد و ہدایت فرماتے رہے" — the **qazi of Ajodhan** was his fierce opponent, but his
  opposition did not work and he went on guiding (pp. 59–60, folios 55–56). **A named office, an unnamed
  holder, opposing the shrine's founder on the spot.**

**Practices & Events**

- **Succession into the seat, and succession out of the family, on one page.** "آپ کے صاحب زادے حضرت بدر
  الدین سلیمان آپ کے سجادہ نشین ہوئے" — his son **بدر الدین سلیمان** became his **sajjada nashin**; "بعض
  نے لکھا ہے کہ آپ کے خلفاء پچاس ہزار سے زیادہ تھے" — some have written that his khalifas were **more than
  fifty thousand** (p. 62, folio 58). **The figure is reported, not asserted.**
- **A second relic transfer by will, after his death.** "آپ کی وفات کے بعد حضرت نظام الدین اولیاء اجودھن
  آئے تو آپ کے داماد حضرت بدر الدین اسحق نے آپ کی وصیت کے مطابق جامہ ان کو دیا" — when Nizam al-Din Auliya
  came to Ajodhan after his death, his **son-in-law** Badr al-Din Ishaq gave him the **جامہ** in accordance
  with his **وصیت**, his will (p. 62, folio 58). **The robe leaves the family by testament while the seat
  stays in it.** On "داماد" against "دادا[OCR?]" see Doubts D13.
- **The succession he received, itemised as an amanat and delivered at a grave on a fixed day.** Qutb
  al-Din Bakhtiar Kaki gave him "اپنا خاص مصلّیٰ اور عصا", his own special prayer-mat and staff, and then
  said: **"میں تمہاری امانت یعنی سجادہ' دستار اور نعلیں جو کہ دست بدست پیران چشت سے مجھ کو پہنچی ہیں' قاضی
  حمید الدین ناگوری کے سپرد کر دوں گا اور جب تم پانچویں روز (وفات) کے ہانسی سے میری قبر پر آؤ گے' وہ یہ
  امانت پیران تم کو پہنچا دیں گے"** — I shall entrust your amanat, that is the **sajjada, the dastar and
  the na'lain**, which reached me **hand to hand (دست بدست) from the pirs of Chisht**, to Qazi Hamid al-Din
  Nagori, and when you come from Hansi to my grave on the **fifth day** after my death, the pirs will
  deliver this trust to you. "حسب فرمان حضرت قطب صاحب رحمۃ اللہ علیہ و تمام تبرکات حضرت قاضی حمید الدین
  ناگوری اور حضرت بدر الدین غزنوی نے آپ کے سپرد کئے" — in accordance with the order, **قاضی حمید الدین
  ناگوری** and **بدر الدین غزنوی** handed over all the **تبرکات**; he wore his pir's khirqa, prayed two
  rak'ats on the musalla and stayed in his pir's house (note 12) (p. 59, folio 55). **Five institutional
  facts at once: the regalia of a seat itemised; "دست بدست" transmission as the claim of legitimacy; named
  trustees holding the relics between two incumbents; a fixed fifth-day handover at the grave; and travel
  from Hansi to Delhi built into the succession. This is the single most useful passage in the range for
  the archive.**
- **A countersigning regime for certificates of succession, run from a second seat.** *(From باب 13.)*
  "حضرت بابا فرید الدین گنج شکر رحمۃ اللہ علیہ جب کسی کو خلافت نامہ عطا فرماتے اس شخص کو تاکید فرماتے کہ
  ہانسی جا کر آپ (شیخ جمال رحمۃ اللہ علیہ) سے مہر لگواؤ' اگر آپ مہر لگا دیتے تو اس کا خلافت نامہ مستند
  سمجھا جاتا اور اگر آپ مہر نہیں لگاتے تو حضرت بابا صاحب بھی قبول نہ فرماتے" — whenever he granted anyone
  a **خلافت نامہ** he enjoined that person to go to **ہانسی** and have it **sealed** by Shaikh Jamal; if he
  sealed it the certificate was reckoned **"مستند"**, authentic; if he did not, Baba Sahib too would not
  accept it, saying **"پارہ کردۂ جمال را نتوانیم دوخت"**, what Jamal has torn we cannot stitch (p. 84,
  folio 80, حاشیہ 4 = سیر الاولیاء فارسی ص 179). **A veto over succession documents vested in a
  subordinate seat.**
- **The regime failing in a single case, and being set aside by the granting shaykh.** *(From باب 12.)*
  Sabir reached Hansi, entered Jamal al-Din's **خانقاہ** still mounted on his **"چنڈول"**, palanquin, and
  dismounted only "[illegible] میں" — **the place is lost, and that is the point at issue in the quarrel**;
  after the prayer Sabir produced "اپنے پیرو مرشد کی عطا کی ہوئی شال", the shawl given by his pir, and
  asked for the seal; a lamp was called for, the wind blew it out, and Jamal al-Din said the certificate
  would be signed tomorrow (pp. 78–79, folios 74–75, حواشی 1–2 = سیر الاقطاب فارسی ص 180). After the lamp
  was relit, "حضرت جمال الدین ہانسوی نے آپ کا خلافت نامہ پھاڑ دیا۔ خلافت نامہ پر مہر نہیں لگائی" — **he
  tore the certificate up and did not seal it** (p. 79, folio 75). Sabir reported the matter at Ajodhan;
  Baba Farid said "پارہ کردہ جمال را فرید نتواں دوخت"; then "حضرت بابا صاحب نے آپ کو کلیر کی ولایت سپرد
  فرمائی' آپ کی مثال پر اپنے دستخط کئے اور اس مرتبہ آپ کو حضرت جمال الدین ہانسوی کے پاس جانے کی تاکید نہیں
  فرمائی" — he entrusted him with the **ولایت of کلیر**, **signed the مثال himself**, and this time did not
  require him to go to Jamal al-Din (pp. 79–80, folios 75–76). **A shrine's grant procedure visibly
  overridden by the granting shaykh.**
- **"ولایت" as a territorial assignment in his gift, three times in the range.** The **ولایت of دہلی** is
  entrusted, with the instruction to get the certificate sealed at Hansi first (p. 78, folio 74); the
  **ولایت of کلیر** replaces it (p. 79, folio 75); and Shams al-Din Turk is told at his leave-taking "کہ
  پانی پت میں مستقل سکونت اختیار کریں' کیوں کہ **پانی پت کی ولایت** ان کے سپرد کی گئی ہے" — to take
  permanent residence at **پانی پت** because the wilayat of Panipat has been entrusted to him (p. 92,
  folio 88). *(The Panipat grant is then quoted from the other side, as the grantee's own claim, at p. 94,
  folio 90 — see Cross-cutting material.)*
- **The regalia of succession he confers, itemised.** To **برہان الدین صوفی**, the minor son of Jamal
  al-Din Hansvi, he "ان کو خرقہ خلافت' مصلا اور عصا عنایت فرمایا" — gave the **khirqa of khilafat, the
  musalla and the asa** — and directed him to spend some time in the service of Nizam al-Din Auliya
  (p. 85, folio 81).
- **Langar distribution as an assigned office at Ajodhan, with a twelve-year term.** "حضرت بابا فرید الدین
  گنج شکر رحمۃ اللہ علیہ نے لنگر تقسیم کرنے کی خدمت آپ کے سپرد فرمائی" — he entrusted Ala al-Din Ahmad
  Sabir with the **service of distributing the langar**; "آپ نے 12 سال کے عرصہ میں کچھ نہیں کھایا", over
  **twelve years** he ate nothing of it; when Baba Farid learned this he said "علاؤ الدین احمد صابر ہیں",
  and from that day the title **"صابر"** stuck (p. 78, folio 74). **A named kitchen office, a named
  office-holder and a stated term.**
- **Food, langar and mendicancy at the shrine town, described in detail** (p. 63, folio 59): he always
  fasted and broke his fast "شربت کے ایک پیالے سے جس میں متقی[OCR?] ہوتی تھی", from one cup of sharbat,
  drinking a little and distributing the rest to those present; of **two روغنی روٹیاں** he ate one and
  broke the second into pieces for the company; when he first came to Ajodhan "آپ اور آپ کے متعلقین **پیلو
  اور ویلہ[OCR?]** پر گزارہ کرتے تھے", he and his dependants subsisted on **pilu** and **vela[OCR?]**;
  **"آپ کے مریدوں نے سالہا سال زنبیل گردان کی ہے"**, his murids carried the **begging bowl** round for
  years; "کھانے میں نمک نہیں ہوتا تھا", there was no salt in the food; "توکل کا یہ حال تھا کہ جو کچھ آتا
  وہ خرچ کر دیتے تھے", whatever came in was spent. **A shrine's founding economy: alms collected by murids,
  wild fruit, and nothing held back.**
- **A land grant refused and cash accepted for redistribution.** "سلطان ناصر الدین جب اوچہ و ملتان گیا تو
  آپ کی خدمت میں حاضر ہوا' واپسی پر اس نے چارہ[OCR?] گاؤں کا فرمان اور کچھ نقدی آپ کی خدمت میں بھیجی۔ **آپ
  نے گاؤں کی مثال واپس کر دی اور نقدی وصول فرما کر درویشوں پر خرچ کی**" — **سلطان ناصر الدین** visited him
  on his way to **اوچہ و ملتان**, Uch and Multan, and afterwards sent a **farman for چارہ[OCR?] villages**
  and some cash; **he returned the villages' deed (مثال) and accepted the cash, spending it on the
  dervishes** (note 16) (p. 61, folio 57). **A precise distinction between a permanent revenue endowment,
  refused, and a one-off cash offering, accepted and immediately spent. The number of villages cannot be
  read; the amount of the cash is (not stated); the sultan is given no dates and no dynasty.**
- **Marriage into the ruling house.** Balban "اس نے اپنی لڑکی بی بی نہرزہ[OCR?] کی شادی آپ سے کی", married
  his daughter to him (p. 61, folio 57), and she is named first among the four wives (p. 62, folio 58).
  **Kinship with the sultanate stated flatly, on the page after the refusal of its land.**
- **Transmission of the ism-e a'zam as lineage property.** Muin al-Din Chishti instructed Qutb Sahib:
  **"اسم اعظم جو خواجگان چشت میں سینہ بہ سینہ چلا آتا ہے' اسے تلقین کرو"** — teach him the Greatest Name
  which has come **breast to breast** among the Khwajgan of Chisht; "آپ پر علم لدنی کا انکشاف ہوا اور
  حجابات کے پردے اٹھ گئے" (p. 61, folio 57). Investiture followed: a **خلعت** from Gharib Nawaz, and the
  **دستار، مثال اور خلافت کے دیگر لوازمات** from Qutb Sahib (p. 61, folio 57).
- **Chilla as an institution of the cell.** Qutb Sahib to Gharib Nawaz: "مسعود … رہ گیا ہے' **وہ چلہ میں
  بیٹھا ہے**", Masud is left out, he is sitting in chilla; the two then went to his **حجرہ** (p. 60,
  folio 56) — with the **چلہ معکوس** (p. 60, folio 56) and the hujra near the Ghazni gate (p. 59,
  folio 55).
- **Sama' endorsed, with a reason.** "آپ کو سماع کا بہت شوق تھا" (p. 63, folio 59), and the saying **"سماع
  راحت دل ہے اور اہل محبت کو جنبش دینے والا ہے' جو بحر محبت میں شناوری کرتا ہے"** — sama' is the heart's
  ease and sets the people of love in motion (p. 65, folio 61). **The book records no objection to it in
  this entry** — the contrast with the saying against sama in باب 1 (p. 27, folio 23) is the range's one
  doctrinal disagreement between two mapped rows.
- **Prescribed aurad and waza'if, one of them for economic relief** (pp. 65–66, folios 61–62): Sura Baqara
  recited between the sunnats and farz of the dawn prayer for three days fulfils a purpose (note 26); Sura
  An'am recited **seventy times** fulfils a need (note 27); "لا الہ الا انت سبحانک انی کنت من الظالمین"
  frees one from grief and worry (note 28); and **"حسب ذیل دعا اکثر پڑھنے سے معاش میں تنگی دور ہوتی ہے"** —
  frequent recitation of the following dua removes **straitness in livelihood** — the text printing it in
  full: "بسم اللّٰہ الرحمٰن یا دائم العز والملک والبقاء یا ذو الجلال و الجود والفضل والعطاء یا و دود ذو
  العرش المجید یا فعال ما یرید" (note 29) (p. 66, folio 62). **A devotional remedy for poverty attached to
  the saint by name — directly usable for what a Pakpattan visitor comes for.**
- **Teachings** (pp. 64–65, folios 60–61): six kinds of **توبہ** — of heart and tongue, eye, ear, hand,
  foot, and nafs (note 21); on the dervish — "درویش جو اس دنیائے دنی کی رفعت و جاہ کا خواستگار ہو … درویش
  نہیں ہے' درویشوں کا نام بد نام کرنے والا ہے اور مرتد طریقت ہے' کیونکہ فقراء کو دنیا سے اعراض ہے", a
  dervish who seeks this base world's eminence is no dervish but one who defames dervishes and an apostate
  from the tariqa (note 22); **"محبت کے ساتھ سو مقام ہیں"**, love has a hundred stations, the first being
  patience under affliction from the Friend (note 23); the **کلاہ** may not be worn until one guards the
  four worlds of eye, ear, tongue, and hand and foot (note 24); and four questions "چار چیزوں کے متعلق سات
  سو پیران طریقت سے پوچھا گیا' سب نے ایک سا جواب دیا", put to **seven hundred pirs of the tariqa**, all of
  whom answered alike — the wisest is he who abandons sins, the most alert he whom nothing troubles, the
  richest the contented, the neediest he who abandons contentment (note 25). Among the aqwal: **"تصوف
  مولیٰ کی صفا دوستی[OCR?] کا نام ہے"** (p. 65, folio 61).
- **Tabarruk distributed at Ajodhan, carried by a qawwal between two seats.** Sabir dismissed **حسن قوال**,
  Hasan Qawwal, with a few **"گولر"**, gular figs; he brought them to Baba Sahib at Ajodhan, "حضرت بابا
  صاحب نے وہ گولر حاضرین کو تقسیم فرمائے", who distributed them to those present, and whoever ate "اس کی
  کثافت دور ہوئی' اس کو نور باطن حاصل ہوا" (pp. 82–83, folios 78–79).
- **A household woman's report of a disciple's renunciation, and its approval.** There was a **کنیز** in
  Jamal al-Din Hansvi's house, "وہ پاک دامن خاتون تھیں", whom Baba Farid addressed as **"مادر مومناں"**,
  mother of the believers; asked "مادر مومناں! جمال ماچہ می کند", she submitted that since meeting Baba
  Sahib "انہوں نے گاؤں' اساب' جائداد اور شغل خطابت کو بالکل ترک کر دیا ہے۔ فاقہ کشی اور محنت پر کمر باندھی
  ہے" — he had wholly abandoned **village, means, estate (جائداد) and the occupation of preaching (شغل
  خطابت)**, girding himself to starvation and labour; Baba Sahib was delighted and said "الحمدللہ' خوش می
  باشد" (pp. 84–85, folios 80–81, حاشیہ 5 = سیر الاولیاء فارسی ص 181).
- **He also appears as father-in-law and maternal kinsman of Sabir**: he married his daughter **"خدیجہ
  بیگم عرف شریفہ"** to him at his mother's insistence (p. 80, folio 76), and Sabir came to Ajodhan with
  his mother and lived with his **ماموں**, maternal uncle, Baba Farid (p. 78, folio 74).
- **He gives khilafat to Shams al-Din Turk but declares it insufficient**, telling him "حصول نعمت و کمال
  تو موقوف بر مرشد دیگر است" — your attainment of the gift and of perfection depends on another murshid —
  and sends him to **کلیر** to Ala al-Din Ahmad Sabir (p. 92, folio 88).

**Legends & Miracles**

- **A child who would not drink, and a fast kept on his account.** There was doubt about the Ramadan moon;
  a buzurg staying there was asked whether to fast and said a child would be born at the qazi's house —
  Farid's father's — and "اگر اس نے دودھ پیا تو روزہ نہیں رکھنا چاہئے"; that night he was born, did not
  drink, and the people fasted (pp. 56–57, folios 52–53).
- **Mud that became sugar — the first reason given for the title "گنج شکر".** Walking to his pir in Delhi
  through the mud, fasting for seven days, his foot slipped and a little mud entered his mouth; "وہ کیچڑ
  خداوند تعالیٰ کے حکم سے شکر ہو گئی", by God's command it became sugar, and his pir said "خداوند تعالیٰ
  نے تیرے وجود کو شکر بنایا' اور خداوند تعالیٰ تجھ کو ہمیشہ میٹھا رکھے گا" (note 6 → تاریخ فرشتہ فارسی
  جلد دوم ص 388) (p. 57, folio 53).
- **Sugar denied, sugar restored — the second reason.** "دوسری وجہ یہ ہے کہ", the second reason is that a
  merchant carrying sugar on bullocks denied it — "نمک ہے شکر نہیں ہے" — and found **salt** in every sack
  at his destination; he came seeking a prayer and was told **"شکر ہو جائے گی"**, it will become sugar, and
  it did (note 7 → اخبار الاخیار اردو ترجمہ ص 109) (p. 57, folio 53). **The book prints both reasons and
  chooses neither.**
- **A single pomegranate seed.** When **جلال الدین تبریزی** met him at **کہتوال**, one seed of the
  pomegranates he had brought was left on the ground; Farid put it in his turban and broke his fast with
  it, felt **انشراح**, expansion, and his pir told him "سب کچھ اسی ایک دانے میں تھا", everything was in
  that one seed (p. 60, folio 56). *(The same episode is told in باب 2 from the other side, with him called
  **"قاضی بچہ"**, Qazi Bacha, living in the **جامع مسجد** at کہتوال[OCR?]: he kept the fallen seed in his
  turban and broke his fast on it, "ان کی طبیعت میں انشراح اور دل میں روشنی پیدا ہوئی" (p. 35, folio 31,
  حاشیہ 7 = اخبار الاخیار اردو ترجمہ ص 87), and at Delhi his pir told him "مسعود! جو انار کا دانہ تیری
  قسمت کا تھا' تجھ کو مل گیا' خاطر جمع رکھو" (p. 36, folio 32, حاشیہ 8). **Chunk 002 recorded this under
  Other saints, not under the row** — see Doubts D1.)*
- **A voice from the unseen accepting him.** After Gharib Nawaz prayed "خدایا! ہمارے فرید کو قبول فرما اور
  اکمل درویش کے مرتبہ پر پہنچا", "غیب سے آواز آئی" — a voice came from the unseen: **"ہم نے فرید کو قبول
  کیا' وہ وحید عصر ہو گا"**, we have accepted Farid, he will be the unique one of the age (note 15 → "معین
  الہند"[OCR?] از ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن شارب ص 98) (pp. 60–61, folios 56–57).
- **A prophecy in the form of a falcon.** Gharib Nawaz to Qutb Sahib: "قطب! بڑے شہباز کو دام میں لائے اس
  کا آشیانہ سدرۃ المنتہی ہو گا" (p. 61, folio 57).
- **Tidings of a throne, fulfilled.** It is said he gave **النمان[OCR?]** the **مژدہ** of crown and throne,
  "چنانچہ وہ بادشاہ ہوا", and accordingly he became king as **غیاث الدین بلبن** (pp. 61–62, folios 57–58).
- **Twenty years upright**, in his own words as the author quotes them (note 14 → راحت القلوب اردو ترجمہ
  ص 300) (p. 60, folio 56).
- **A dervish drowned after being refused a comb.** A dervish asked three times for the comb on the
  musalla and at last said "کنگھی مجھ کو دو' تمہارے واسطے برکت ہو گی"; the saint answered **"جاؤ' میرے حال
  میں دخل نہ دو' تجھ کو اور تیری برکت کو میں نے آب رواں میں ڈال دیا"** — go, do not meddle in my state; I
  have cast you and your blessing into running water (note 30 → تاریخ فرشتہ فارسی ص 385); **outside
  Ajodhan** the dervish bathed in the river, could not come out, "ڈوب کر مر گیا", drowned and died
  (note 31 → خیر المجالس اردو ترجمہ ص 155) (p. 66, folio 62).
- **Dates that became pure gold.** Six dervishes asked for travel provisions and were given the dates
  lying before him; they were displeased and made to throw them away, and as they looked saw "وہ خرمے زر
  خالص کے ہو گئے ہیں" (note 32 → راحت القلوب اردو ترجمہ ص 151) (p. 66, folio 62).
- **Magic survived and forgiven**: the son of **شہاب الدین ساحر** worked magic on him, "آپ نے اس کا قصور
  معاف کیا" (p. 60, folio 56).

**Significance**

- **The chapter's source base is the broadest in the range, and mixed in language.** **سیر الاولیاء
  (فارسی)** carries eight of the thirty-two notes (one shared with two other titles), **تاریخ فرشتہ** six,
  and the Urdu translations **راحت القلوب** and **اسرار الاولیاء** five each; also جواہر فریدی ص 119،
  انوار العارفین (فارسی) ص 292، روضۃ الاقطاب (فارسی) ص 59، اخبار الاخیار، سفینۃ الاولیاء، خیر المجالس and
  فوائد القوائد as printed (pp. 67–68, folios 63–64). **Two notes cite the book's own author**: "دلی کے
  (22) بائیس خواجہ از ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن شارب ص 38" (note 10) and "معین الہند"[OCR?] از ڈاکٹر ظہور الحسن
  شارب ص 98 (note 15) (p. 67, folio 63).
- **The author stacks reports and does not adjudicate**: two birth years (notes 3–4), two death years
  (note 18), two explanations of "گنج شکر", two origins for the name Farid al-Din, a khalifa count as
  "بعض نے لکھا ہے", three of four attributed risalas as merely "بتائی جاتی ہیں" (pp. 56–63, folios 52–59).
  **There is no editorial voice correcting the text anywhere in the chapter.**
- **His sayings are presented as usable religious technology**: the اوراد و وظائف rubric turns the saint's
  teaching into numbered prescriptions with stated effects — a need fulfilled, grief removed, livelihood
  eased (pp. 65–66, folios 61–62).
- **Evaluation is formulaic rather than argued**: "آپ کی تعلیمات تصوف کا بیش بہا خزانہ ہیں", his teachings
  are a priceless treasury of tasawwuf (p. 64, folio 60).

---

### data-darbar — حضرت شیخ علی ہجویری, "Hazrat Shaikh Ali Hujwiri", as باب 1 heads him (p. 21, folio 17); the name alone, "آپ کا نام 'علی' ہے", Ali (p. 21, folio 17); "علی ہجویری", Ali Hujwiri (p. 21, folio 17), spoken by the figure himself as "میں علی ہجویری ہوں", "I am Ali Hujwiri" (p. 251, folio 247); kunya "ابو الحسن", Abu al-Hasan (p. 21, folio 17, حاشیہ 2); laqab "داتا گنج بخش", Data Ganj Bakhsh (p. 21, folio 17), shortened by some to "گنج بخش", Ganj Bakhsh (p. 22, folio 18); takhallus as a poet "علی", Ali (p. 23, folio 19); the site as "مزار پر انوار", the radiant mazar (p. 23, folio 19; p. 250, folio 246) and as "حضرت داتا گنج بخش رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار", the mazar of Hazrat Data Ganj Bakhsh (pp. 250, 251; folios 246, 247)

- **Saint.** **The two ranges are grossly unequal and the reader must see it.** In pp. 1–109 the book treats him at chapter length: باب 1 is the book's first chapter, runs pp. 21–29 (folios 17–25), is complete within that range, and carries twenty numbered حواشی printed together at p. 29 (folio 25), supporting every substantive claim except two (p. 29, folio 25). In pp. 211–303 he is engaged **only** in chunk 012, pp. 250–251 (folios 246–247), inside باب 50, and there only as the shrine another saint is ordered to attend — a single episode, not a treatment (pp. 250–251, folios 246–247).
  - **Titles.** "سلطان الطریقت ہیں۔ گنج حقیقت ہیں۔ برہان الشریعت ہیں" — Sultan al-Tariqat, Ganj al-Haqiqat, Burhan al-Shariat (p. 21, folio 17).
  - **Descent.** "آپ سادات حسنی سے ہیں" — of the **Hasani sayyids**; the lineage runs "چند واسطوں سے", through a few intermediaries, to "حضرت سیدنا زید شہید رضی اللہ عنہ ابن امیر المومنین حضرت سیدنا امام حسن" — Sayyidna **Zaid Shahid** son of Amir al-Muminin **Imam Hasan** (p. 21, folio 17). The number of intermediaries is **(not stated)** (p. 21, folio 17).
  - **Place of origin and family.** "آپ کے آباؤ اجداد غزنی کے رہنے والے تھے" — his forefathers were residents of **غزنی**, Ghazni; his grandfather's name was "حضرت علی", Ali (p. 21, folio 17). Father: "آپ کے والد ماجد کا نام عثمان ہے" — **عثمان**, Usman (p. 21, folio 17, حاشیہ 1, citing کشف المحجوب اردو[OCR?] ترجمہ ص 2 and سفینۃ الاولیاء فارسی ص 164). The pp. 1–109 worker records that this agrees with the archive row's "Ali ibn Usman al-Hujwiri" (p. 21, folio 17, حاشیہ 1).
  - **Birth.** "آپ غزنی میں پیدا ہوئے" — born in **Ghazni** (p. 21, folio 17). Year of birth: **(not stated)** (p. 21, folio 17).
  - **What "Hujwiri" means, asserted without a source.** "ہجویر شہر کا نام نہیں ہے، بلکہ غزنی کے ایک محلے کا نام ہے۔ اس لئے آپ 'علی ہجویری' کہلاتے ہیں" — **Hujwir is not the name of a city but the name of a quarter (محلہ) of Ghazni**, and that is why he is called Ali Hujwiri (p. 21, folio 17). **Placement caution: the book asserts this without citing a source** — one of only two unfootnoted substantive claims in the chapter (p. 21, folio 17; p. 29, folio 25).
  - **Laqab, and its stated origin.** "آپ 'داتا گنج بخش' کے لقب سے پکارے جاتے ہیں" — he is called by the laqab **داتا گنج بخش**, Data Ganj Bakhsh (p. 21, folio 17); "بعض لوگ تو آپ کو 'گنج بخش' کے لقب سے پکارا کرتے ہیں", some call him simply Ganj Bakhsh (p. 22, folio 18). The book gives the laqab a **posthumous** origin, conferred at the tomb by another saint (pp. 21–22, folios 17–18, حاشیہ 3) — see Legends & Miracles.
  - **Training.** "آپ علوم ظاہری کی تحصیل سے فارغ ہو کر علوم باطنی کی طرف متوجہ ہوئے" — having finished the outward sciences he turned to the inward (p. 22, folio 18). Teacher (استاد): **شیخ ابو القاسم**, Shaikh Abu al-Qasim, whose saying on attendance upon a murshid is quoted: a murshid "وہ ہوتا ہے جو کہ غواص ہو نہ کہ متصل [OCR?]", is one who is a **diver** and not متصل[OCR?]; and one should take bai'at only "اگر اپنے میں قوت پائے", if one finds strength in oneself, "اور اپنے میں قوت نہ ہو تو دونوں خراب ہوں گے", and if not, both will be ruined (p. 22, folio 18, حاشیہ 4, citing نفحات الانس فارسی ص 291).
  - **Bai'at and khilafat, a three-link chain.** "آپ شیخ ابو الفضل بن حسن ختلی کے مرید ہیں" — murid of **شیخ ابو الفضل بن حسن ختلی**, Shaikh Abu al-Fazl bin Hasan Khuttali (حاشیہ 5, سفینۃ الاولیاء فارسی ص 164); "وہ مرید حضرت خضری [OCR?] کے، اور وہ مرید حضرت شیخ شبلی کے ہیں" — he the murid of **حضرت خضری**[OCR?], and he the murid of **حضرت شیخ شبلی**, Shaikh Shibli (p. 22, folio 18, حاشیہ 6, same source).
  - **Silsila: (not stated), in both ranges.** **No silsila is named as an order anywhere in باب 1** (pp. 21–29, folios 17–25), and the pp. 211–303 mention likewise records **silsila: (not stated)** (pp. 250–251, folios 246–247). The archive row's `silsila` field is empty and the book supplies nothing to fill it (pp. 21–29, folios 17–25; pp. 250–251, folios 246–247).
  - **Travels and other affiliations.** "آپ نے خراسان، ماوراء النہر، مرو، آذربائیجان وغیرہ کی سیر و سیاحت فرمائی" — **خراسان** Khurasan, **ماوراء النہر** Transoxiana, **مرو** Marw, **آذربائیجان** Azerbaijan and others; he met many darwishes and drew benefit from the spiritual graces of **حضرت شیخ ابو القاسم گرگانی**, **حضرت شیخ ابو سعید ابو الخیر** and **حضرت شیخ ابو القاسم قشیری** (p. 22, folio 18, حاشیہ 7, citing کشف الاسرار اردو ترجمہ ص 16).
  - **UNRECONCILED — two accounts of how he reached India, left standing side by side and adjudicated by neither the book nor this merge.** (a) "بعض لوگوں کا خیال ہے کہ آپ سلطان مسعود بن سلطان محمود غزنوی کے لشکر کے ساتھ ہندوستان تشریف لائے" — some think he came to Hindustan with the army of **سلطان مسعود بن سلطان محمود غزنوی**, Sultan Masud son of Sultan Mahmud Ghaznavi (pp. 22–23, folios 18–19, حاشیہ 8). (b) "اور بعض لوگ اس سے متفق نہیں، ان کا کہنا ہے کہ آپ شیخ احمد جماوی [OCR?] سرخسی اور شیخ ابو سعید ہجویری کے ہمراہ ہندوستان تشریف لائے" — and some do not agree, saying he came in the company of **شیخ احمد جماوی**[OCR?] **سرخسی** and **شیخ ابو سعید ہجویری** (pp. 22–23, folios 18–19, حاشیہ 8, citing فوائد الفوائد اردو ترجمہ ص 27). **The book adjudicates neither** (pp. 22–23, folios 18–19).
  - **Arrival at Lahore and the funeral he met.** He stayed outside the city because night had fallen; entering in the morning he saw a bier and on enquiry learned "کہ حضرت شیخ حسن زنجانی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کا جنازہ ہے", that it was the janaza of **حضرت شیخ حسن زنجانی**, Shaikh **Hasan** Zanjani (p. 23, folio 19, حاشیہ 9, citing کشف الاسرار اردو ترجمہ ص 16). The pp. 1–109 input flags this against the archive under its Doubts D2, on Zanjani (p. 23, folio 19).
  - **Settlement.** "آپ نے لاہور میں مستقل سکونت اختیار فرمائی اور تعلیم و تلقین اور رشد و ہدایت میں مشغول ہوئے" — he took **permanent residence in لاہور, Lahore**, and occupied himself in teaching, talqin and guidance (p. 23, folio 19).
  - **UNRECONCILED — two death years printed side by side.** (a) "آپ نے **465ھ** میں اس جہاں فانی سے سفر دار الاخرت فرمایا" — he journeyed to the abode of the hereafter in **465ھ**, 465 AH (p. 23, folio 19, حاشیہ 10, citing سفینۃ الاولیاء فارسی ص 16). (b) "بعض کے نزدیک آپ کا وصال **456ھ** میں ہوا" — in the view of some his passing was in **456ھ**, 456 AH (p. 23, folio 19, حاشیہ 11, citing کشف الاسرار اردو ترجمہ ص 16). **The book takes no side and neither does this merge**; both years are printed in Western digits (p. 23, folio 19). **Day and month: (not stated). Age at death: (not stated)** (p. 23, folio 19). No Common-Era equivalent is printed and none is computed here; the pp. 1–109 input flags the relation to the archive row's `1072` under its Doubts D3 (p. 23, folio 19). The pp. 211–303 mention records **death year: (not stated)**, so that the row's "1072" is there "neither confirmed nor contradicted" (pp. 250–251, folios 246–247) — silence, not a third reading.
  - **Character.** "آپ قطب زمانہ تھے" — he was the **qutb of the age**; engaged in zikr and fikr, muraqaba and muhasaba, ibadat and riyazat; "دنیاوی آلائش سے پاک و صاف تھے", clean of worldly taint; "تصرفات آپ کے بے شمار ہیں", his tasarrufat are countless (p. 23, folio 19).
  - **Works, six titles, three of them OCR-flagged.** "آپ نے بہت سے کتابیں لکھی ہیں": **منہاج الدین**, **البیان الاہل العیان**[OCR?], **اسر الخرق و المونیات**[OCR?], **کشف الاسرار**, **الرعایۃ بحوق اللہ**[OCR?], **کشف المحجوب** (p. 23, folio 19, حاشیہ 12, citing کشف الاسرار اردو ترجمہ ص 12). **Three of the six are OCR-flagged and cannot be identified from the notes** (p. 23, folio 19).
  - **Poetry.** "آپ شاعر بھی تھے۔ آپ کا تخلص 'علی' ہے۔ آپ صاحب دیوان ہیں" — he was also a poet, his takhallus is **علی**, and he possesses a diwan, as he says himself: "میں نے بیت اور اشعار بہت سے کہے ہیں اور ایک دیوان بھی کہا...." (p. 23, folio 19, حاشیہ 13, citing کشف المحجوب اردو ترجمہ ص 3). A Persian **غزل** attributed to him, seven couplets, is printed at p. 24 (folio 20) but is **too damaged to quote**, flagged under the pp. 1–109 input's Doubts D18 (p. 24, folio 20).
  - **The figure names himself at the mazar, in the pp. 211–303 range.** At the mazar "ایک نورانی صورت نمودار ہوئی" — a luminous form appeared — which asked whether he knew who it was and then said "میں علی ہجویری ہوں", "I am Ali Hujwiri" (p. 251, folio 247). The pp. 211–303 worker records that the book's two designations there, "داتا گنج بخش" and "علی ہجویری", match the archive row's principal figure "Hazrat Ali ibn Usman al-Hujwiri (Data Ganj Bakhsh)" (pp. 250–251, folios 246–247).

- **Shrine / Darbar.** Both ranges together yield one locational sentence and a set of naming formulas; everything else about the site is **(not stated)**.
  - **The only statement about the shrine as a place in باب 1**: "مزار پر انوار لاہور میں فیوض و برکات کا سرچشمہ ہے" — the radiant mazar **in لاہور, Lahore**, is a fountainhead of graces and blessings (p. 23, folio 19). This is also one of the two unfootnoted substantive claims in the chapter (p. 23, folio 19; p. 29, folio 25).
  - **Naming of the site in the pp. 211–303 range.** The shrine is named four times as "حضرت داتا گنج بخش رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار" (pp. 250, 251; folios 246, 247), twice with the epithet "مزار پر انوار", the radiant mazar (p. 250, folio 246).
  - **Placement caution, carried from the pp. 211–303 input: that chunk never prints a town beside this mazar**, and its Lahore placement is contextual only (pp. 250–251, folios 246–247). The Lahore placement is printed only at p. 23 (folio 19), in the other range.
  - **(not stated) throughout both ranges**: no quarter, street, locality or building description; no urs date; no custodian or sajjada nashin; no Auqaf; no endowment; no visitor numbers; no dispute over the darbar (pp. 21–29, folios 17–25; pp. 250–251, folios 246–247).
  - **The mosque he built at Lahore is the only building in باب 1, and it is not the shrine** (pp. 27–28, folios 23–24) — see Practices & Events.

- **Practices & Events.** The pp. 1–109 material is all doctrine, teaching and one building; the pp. 211–303 material is all about attendance at the mazar itself.
  - **A mosque built at Lahore by Ali Hujwiri, with a disputed qibla.** "لاہور میں سکونت اختیار کرنے کے بعد آپ نے ایک مسجد تعمیر کرانا شروع کی۔ مسجد کی سمت قبلہ رو نہ ہونے کی وجہ سے علماء نے اعتراض کیا" — after settling in Lahore he began to have a mosque built, and the **ulama objected** that its direction was not qibla-facing; "اس مسجد کی محراب جنوب کی طرف معلوم ہوتی تھی", the mihrab appeared to face **south**; he answered none of the objections and the work continued (p. 27, folio 23). When it was finished "آپ نے علماء و مشائخ کو بلایا' سب نے وہاں نماز پڑھی' آپ نے امامت فرمائی" — he summoned the **ulama and mashaikh**, all prayed there and **he led the prayer** (p. 28, folio 24, حاشیہ 20, citing سفینۃ الاولیاء فارسی ص 364). **No date, cost, patron or site within Lahore is given** (pp. 27–28, folios 23–24).
  - **A doctrine that a faqir must avoid rulers.** "فقیر کو چاہیے کہ بادشاہوں اور حاکموں کی آشنائی و معرفت کو سخت خطرناک سانپوں اور بڑے بھاری اژدہاؤں کی معرفت و آشنائی سمجھے اور جانے۔ کیوں کہ جب بادشاہ کا تقرب فقیر کو حاصل ہوا تو جانے کہ اس کا زاد اور توشہ برباد ہو گیا" — a faqir should reckon acquaintance with **kings and rulers** as acquaintance with venomous snakes and great dragons, because once a faqir gains a king's favour let him know his provision and viaticum are ruined; and "جب ترکی ٹوپی تو نے سر پر رکھ لی تو اس سے کچھ فقیری حاصل نہیں ہوتی", putting a Turkish cap on your head gains you no faqiri (p. 25, folio 21, حاشیہ 16, citing کشف الاسرار اردو ترجمہ ص 3). The pp. 1–109 worker reads this as **a principled refusal of state patronage, stated by the saint of Data Darbar himself** (p. 25, folio 21).
  - **A saying against sama.** "مبتدی[OCR?] کو چاہیے کہ وہ **سماع** کے نزدیک نہ جائے اور اس سے کنارہ کش رہے" — a beginner[OCR?] should not go near **sama** and should keep away from it (p. 27, folio 23). The pp. 1–109 worker notes it is **relevant to any claim about musical practice at the darbar, and that it is a restriction, not a licence** (p. 27, folio 23).
  - **Charity argued from the grave.** "دولت کو ایک عذاب جان اور اس کو اہل فاقہ لوگوں کو دیدے اور تصدق کر' کیونکہ آخر قبر میں کیڑے کھائیں گے اور اگر تو نے یہ بخشش میں دی دے تو وہ تیرے دوستدار رہیں گے" — reckon wealth a torment, give it to the starving and give it as **sadaqa**, for in the end worms will eat it in the grave, whereas if you give it away as a gift they will remain your friends (pp. 26–27, folios 22–23).
  - **Wird.** "اللہ تعالیٰ کے اسماء حسنیٰ میں سے اسم **یا حبیب** اور **یا لطیف** کے معنوں کو اپنے وجود میں رکھ لے اور ان کو اپنا ورد بنا لے" — take the meanings of the names **Ya Habib** and **Ya Latif** into your being and make them your wird (p. 27, folio 23, حاشیہ 19, citing کشف الاسرار اردو ترجمہ ص 4). **The only wird prescribed for him in the pp. 1–109 range** (p. 27, folio 23).
  - **Teachings, five headed sections plus ten aqwal** (pp. 24–27, folios 20–23): **نفسانی غرض**, self-interest, which lifts the blessing from any work (حاشیہ 14, کشف المحجوب ص 48، 49); **صوفیوں کی صحبت**, the company of the Sufis, a fourfold analysis of the motives of those who seek it, ending on those who "اپنی آفتوں کو ان کی اصلاح میں چھپائیں اور ان کا جامہ پہنیں", hide their own afflictions in the Sufis' correction and wear their garb (حاشیہ 15, کشف المحجوب ص 222); **مجاہدہ نفس**, the struggle against the self, which "رسول اللہ صلی اللہ علیہ وسلم نے … جہاد پر بزرگ رکھا ہے", the Prophet held greater than jihad; **فقیر**, on rulers (above); **دس چیزیں**, ten things that devour ten (حاشیہ 17); and **آٹھ کلمات**, eight sayings selected from Luqman Hakim (حاشیہ 18) (pp. 24–27, folios 20–23).
  - **The shrine as a place of assigned, timed service.** Attendance there is ordered by a pir — "کہ حضرت داتا گنج بخش رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار پر انوار پر پابندی سے حاضر ہوا کریں" (p. 250, folio 246) — performed "بارہ برس تک نہایت پابندی سے", **twelve years, very strictly**, and answered by the figure at the shrine with "یہ اس خدمت کا صلہ ہے، جو تم نے بارہ سال کی ہے" — this is the reward of the service you have done for twelve years (p. 251, folio 247). The pp. 211–303 worker reads this as **attendance at a shrine framed as an assigned term of service with a stated payoff** (pp. 250–251, folios 246–247).
  - **What is conferred there.** The form "آپ کو نعمت باطنی سے مالا مال کر دیا، آپ کو ولایت عطا فرمائی اور شراب وحدت سے مدہوش و سرشار کیا" — enriched him with inward bounty, **granted him ولایت, wilayat**, and made him drunk with the wine of unity (p. 251, folio 247).
  - **One of two places where a saint found stillness**: "دریا کے کنارے اور حضرت داتا گنج بخش رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے مزار پر سکون' یک سوئی پاتے" (p. 250, folio 246), and a fixed station in a daily timetable between the dawn prayer and the maktab (pp. 250–251, folios 246–247).

- **Legends & Miracles.**
  - **The couplet that made the laqab, conferred posthumously at the tomb by another saint.** When **حضرت خواجہ معین الدین حسن چشتی سنجری** came to Lahore "تو آپ کے مزار مبارک پر اعتکاف فرمایا", he performed **اعتکاف, itikaf**, at Hujwiri's blessed mazar, and on leaving recited "گنج بخش فیض عالم مظہر نور خدا / ناقصاں را پیر کامل کاملاں را رہنما"; "اس روز سے آپ داتا گنج بخش مشہور ہوئے", from that day he became famous as Data Ganj Bakhsh (pp. 21–22, folios 17–18, حاشیہ 3, citing کشف الاسرار اردو ترجمہ ص 8). The pp. 1–109 worker records this as **the only act of devotion performed at the shrine in that range, and the origin the book gives for the shrine's name** (pp. 21–22, folios 17–18) — the pp. 211–303 range supplies a second such act at pp. 250–251 (folios 246–247).
  - **The dream that assigned Lahore, with no source footnoted.** "مرشد کے حکم سے ہندوستان تشریف لائے" — he came to Hindustan by his murshid's command; one night he saw his pir in a dream saying "تم کو لاہور کا قطب بنایا گیا ہے۔ لاہور تمہارے سپرد کیا گیا ہے۔ لاہور جاؤ" — you have been made the **qutb of Lahore**, Lahore has been entrusted to you, go to Lahore. He objected in the dream that Khwaja Hasan Zanjani was already there, "ان کی موجودگی میں میرا لاہور جانا بے کار ہے", and the pir replied there was no need for argument and he should set out quickly (p. 22, folio 18). **No source is footnoted for the dream** (p. 22, folio 18). The pp. 1–109 worker adds the observation — **an inference of the chunk worker, not a statement of the book** — that the institutional form is a city assigned as a jurisdiction to one saint at a time, the same form as the "ولایت" grants at pp. 78–79, 92, 94 (folios 74–75, 88, 90).
  - **The wisdom of the command shown by a funeral.** Meeting Hasan Zanjani's bier on the morning he entered Lahore, "اب آپ کو معلوم ہوا کہ پیرو مرشد کے فرمان میں کیا مصلحت تھی" — now he understood what the expediency in his pir's command had been (p. 23, folio 19, حاشیہ 9).
  - **The mosque whose qibla was proved by lifting the veils.** After the congregation had prayed he asked those present to consider where the Kaaba lay; "آپ نے توجہ فرمائی' حجابات اٹھ گئے' سب نے دیکھا کہ کعبہ سامنے اور مسجد کی سمت صحیح ہے" — he directed his attention, the veils were lifted, and all saw the **Kaaba in front of them** and the mosque's direction correct (p. 28, folio 24, حاشیہ 20, citing سفینۃ الاولیاء فارسی ص 364). The pp. 1–109 worker records this as **the book's only karamat of Hujwiri in that range, settling an architectural dispute by vision rather than by instrument** (p. 28, folio 24).
  - **A luminous form at the mazar naming itself**, granting **ولایت**, wilayat, and the wine of unity, and telling the attendant this was the reward of twelve years' service (p. 251, folio 247).
  - Of Hujwiri generally the author says only "تصرفات آپ کے بے شمار ہیں" — his tasarrufat are countless (p. 23, folio 19) — and names none beyond the mosque in that range (pp. 21–29, folios 17–25).

- **Significance.**
  - **باب 1 is a hagiography with a citation apparatus resting on a narrow base.** Its twenty حواشی cite **six works only** — کشف المحجوب (اردو[OCR?] ترجمہ), سفینۃ الاولیاء (فارسی), خزینۃ الاصفیاء, کشف الاسرار (اردو ترجمہ; once "ردو[OCR?] ترجمہ"), نفحات الانس (فارسی), فوائد الفوائد (اردو ترجمہ) — four of them read in Urdu translation and two in Persian; **nine of the twenty point to a single book**, کشف الاسرار (اردو ترجمہ), and three to سفینۃ الاولیاء at three different pages (ص 16، 164، 364) (p. 29, folio 25).
  - **Every substantive claim about the saint carries a note except two**: the Hujwir-is-a-quarter etymology (p. 21, folio 17) and the single mazar sentence (p. 23, folio 19) (p. 29, folio 25).
  - **The chapter's institutional yield for the archive is close to nil and its silence is systematic**, not accidental: the author's stated programme (p. 18) contains no shrine, urs, custodianship or endowment category, and the entry gives the mazar one line (p. 23, folio 19).
  - **In the pp. 211–303 range the shrine is the source of another saint's wilayat** (p. 251, folio 247) — the pp. 211–303 worker records it as the only place in that range where a shrine, rather than a living pir, confers **ولایت** (p. 251, folio 247).

### shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam — حضرت شیخ رکن الدین ابو الفتح (باب 17)

Subject of باب 17 (pp. 113–120, folios 109–116) and a recurring authority in باب 22 (pp. 133–138,
folios 129–134).

**Basis of the identification, which rests on the book's statements and not on the name.** "آپ حضرت
شیخ بہاء الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ ملتانی کے پوتے ہیں" — he is the grandson of Shaikh Baha al-Din
Zakariya Multani (p. 113, folio 109); his mazar is at **ملتان** (p. 116, folio 112); he inherits
"حضرت شیخ شہاب الدین سہروردی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کا خرقہ" — the khirqa of Shaikh Shihab al-Din Suhrawardi —
from his father (p. 114, folio 110). A second, independent locational statement comes from باب 22:
"ملتان میں آپ شیخ رکن الدین ابو الفتح کی اجازت سے مدرسے میں رہتے تھے" — in **ملتان** Makhdum Jahaniyan
lived in the madrasa by his permission (p. 134, folio 130), which places him at Multan with authority
over a teaching institution. **chunk_004 left this figure unmapped for want of exactly that locational
statement; the mapping is offered here with that history on the record** (p. 134, folio 130).

**Saint**

- Opening encomium: "حضرت شیخ رکن الدین ابو الفتح خورشید سپہر ہدایت ہیں" — he is the sun of the sky of
  guidance (p. 113, folio 109).
- **Name, kunya, laqab**: "آپ کے دادا حضرت بہاؤ الدین زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ نے آپ کا نام رکن الدین
  رکھا" — his grandfather named him **رکن الدین** (p. 113, folio 109); kunya "**ابو الفتح**"; "آپ کا
  لقب 'فضل اللہ' ہے" — his laqab is **فضل اللہ** (p. 113, folio 109). **The book never uses the title
  "رکن عالم" / Rukn-e-Alam anywhere in this range** (p. 113, folio 109; see Doubts).
- **Birth**: "آپ نے 9 رمضان 649ھ کو بروز جمعہ اس عالم کو زینت بخشی" — **9 Ramadan 649 AH, a Friday**
  (p. 113, folio 109).
- **Father — two names, unreconciled.** The rubric "والد ماجد" gives "آپ کے والد ماجد کا نام رکن الدین
  [OCR?] ہے" — his father's name is **رکن الدین [OCR?]**, which would make father and son homonyms
  (p. 113, folio 109) — while the narrative twice names the father **حضرت شیخ صدر الدین عارف**, once in
  the turban story and once under "بیعت و خلافت" (p. 114, folio 110). The rubric reading is
  **OCR-flagged**; the narrative reading is not. **Both stand; the book does not reconcile them.**
- **Mother**: "آپ کی والدہ ماجد بی بی راستی رابعہ عصر اور حافظہ کلام اللہ تھیں' وہ حضرت بہاء الدین
  زکریا رحمۃ اللہ علیہ ملتانی کی مرید تھیں" — his mother **بی بی راستی** (Bibi Rasti), the Rabia of the
  age and a hafiza of the Quran, was a murid of Baha al-Din Zakariya Multani; "روزانہ ایک قرآن ختم کرنا
  ان کا معمول تھا", completing one Quran daily was her practice (p. 113, folio 109). She is both
  daughter-in-law and murid of the grandfather, whom the text calls her "**خسر**", father-in-law
  (p. 113, folio 109).
- **Prediction before birth**: standing for his pregnant daughter-in-law, Baha al-Din Zakariya said
  "یہ تعظیم اس بچے کی ہے۔ جو تیرے پیٹ میں ہے۔ وہ چراغ خاندان و شمع دو دمان کا ہو گا" — this respect is
  for the child in your womb; he will be the lamp of the family and the candle of two houses (p. 113,
  folio 109, footnote 1 → "سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 527"); the child was then "سات ماہ کے اپنی والدہ
  کے شکم مبارک میں", seven months in the womb (p. 113, folio 109).
- **Education** under his grandfather's supervision, "تعلیم و تربیت" (p. 114, folio 110).
- **Bai'at and khilafat**: "آپ اپنے والد ماجد حضرت شیخ صدر الدین عارف کے مرید اور خلیفہ اعظم ہیں" —
  murid and **خلیفہ اعظم** of his father Shaikh Sadr al-Din Arif; after his father's passing he became
  **سجادہ نشین** and occupied himself with teaching, talqin and guidance (p. 114, folio 110).
- **Brother**: **شیخ عماد الدین اسمٰعیل** (pp. 115–116, folios 111–112).
- **No children**: "آپ کے کوئی اولاد نہیں تھی" (p. 116, folio 112).
- **Death**: "وفات سے تین ماہ قبل گوشہ تنہائی اختیار کر لی تھی۔ 16' رجب 735ھ کو جوار رحمت میں داخل
  ہوئے" — three months before his death he took to seclusion; he entered the neighbourhood of mercy on
  **16 Rajab 735 AH** (p. 116, folio 112; footnote 9 → "سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 527", p. 120,
  folio 116). **No CE equivalent is given anywhere** (see Doubts on the archive's 1335).
- **Sirat, quantified by age**: bound to prayer and fasting from **seven**; prayed in congregation; kept
  tahajjud, ishraq, chasht and nawafil; fasted in **Ashura of Muharram** besides Ramadan; zikr khafi and
  jali, muraqaba and muhasaba among his practices (p. 116, folio 112). "کشف قلوب' طے ارض و طے لسان میں
  دس سال کی عمر سے ممتاز تھے' کمالات صوری و معنوی آپ کو پچیس سال کی عمر سے حاصل تھے" — distinguished in
  kashf-e qulub, tai-e arz and tai-e lisan from the age of **ten**, and possessed of the formal and
  inward perfections from **twenty-five** (p. 116, folio 112).
- Habit: "خوراک آپ کی کم تھی' لیکن کھانا اچھا کھاتے تھے" — he ate little but ate well (p. 116,
  folio 112).
- **Teachings** (rubric تعلیمات): on "صورت اور صفت", form and attribute — judgement rests on attribute,
  not form, and its verification appears only in the abode of the hereafter, where each person is
  gathered in the form answering to his attribute (p. 117, folio 113, footnote 14 → "مجمع الاخبار"); and
  on "اعمال پر متابعت", holding the limbs back from what the sharia forbids or dislikes, in word and
  deed, and avoiding idle assemblies and the company of "بطالوں" (pp. 117–118, folios 113–114).
- **A question of doctrine put by his brother.** Shaikh Imad al-Din Ismail asked "حضرت محمد مصطفٰی صلی
  اللہ علیہ وسلم کی ہجرت میں کیا حکمت تھی؟" — what was the wisdom in the Prophet's hijra; Rukn al-Din's
  answer turned on the "اصحاب صفہ" and Nizam al-Din's on the poor of Medina who could not otherwise
  reach the Prophet's company (p. 115, folio 111).

**Shrine / Darbar**

- "مزار پر انوار ملتان میں مرجع خاص و عام ہے" — the luminous mazar at **ملتان** is a resort of high and
  low (p. 116, folio 112).
- **No quarter, no building date, no urs, no custodian, no endowment, no land, no jagir, no waqf and no
  visitor figure is stated for the shrine itself anywhere in this range** — "(not stated)" (p. 116,
  folio 112; and nowhere in pp. 133–138, folios 129–134).
- **His mazar is visited by others**, third of the three mazars of an Eid-night vigil: "پھر صدر الدین
  عارف اور [OCR?] حضرت رکن الدین ابو الفتح کے مزارات پر حاضر ہوئے" (p. 133, folio 129). **The town of
  those three mazars is (not stated) in that sentence**, and the `[OCR?]` flag sits on the word "اور"
  joining the two names, so it is not certain whether a third name or a title is lost there (p. 133,
  folio 129).

**Practices & Events**

- **A succession enacted as the handing over of a turban, and a khirqa worn at the accession.** At four
  years old, his grandfather's "دستار" lay on the bed-leg; playing, he lifted it onto his own head; his
  father Sadr al-Din Arif forbade him; Baha al-Din Zakariya answered "بابا صدر الدین! تم اس کو منع نہ
  کرو' کیوں کہ اس نے دستار بطور حق کے اپنے سر پر رکھی ہے' میں نے یہ دستار اس کو دی" — do not forbid him,
  for he has set the turban on his head **as of right**; I have given him this turban. And then:
  "اپنے والد کی وفات کے بعد جب آپ سجادہ نشین ہوئے تو آپ نے وہی دستار سر پر رکھی اور حضرت شیخ شہاب الدین
  سہروردی رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کا خرقہ جو آپ کو والد سے ملا تھا' زیب تن فرمایا" — on becoming **سجادہ نشین**
  after his father's death he put **that same turban** on his head and donned the **khirqa of Shaikh
  Shihab al-Din Suhrawardi** which had come to him from his father (p. 114, folio 110, footnotes 2–3 →
  "سیر العارفین"; "سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 527"). **Two heritable objects and a grandfather's
  pre-emptive designation over the sitting successor** — the clearest account in this range of how a
  Multan sajjada was transmitted.
- **Lateral succession after him, and a khalifa list as an institutional record.** "آپ کی وفات کے بعد
  آپ کے بھائی حضرت عماد الدین اسمٰعیل آپ کے سجادہ نشین ہوئے" — his **brother** Imad al-Din Ismail became
  sajjada nashin, he himself having no children; six "ممتاز خلفاء" follow (p. 116, folio 112,
  footnote 10 → "سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 523").
- **Named khalifas** (p. 116, folio 112): حضرت سید جلال الدین بخاری ملقب بہ مخدوم جہانیاں جہاں گشت;
  حضرت شیخ عثمان سیاح; حضرت حاجی شیخ صدر الدین عارف; حاجی چراغ دین; حضرت شیخ حمید الدین ملقب بہ سلطان
  التارکین قرشی المہنکاری [OCR?]. None is given a place or a date. A further khalifa, **حضرت شیخ قوام
  الدین**, is named in باب 22 (p. 135, folio 131).
- **State cash on a scale, and the protocol of the third gate.** "سلطان علاء الدین آپ کا بہت معتقد تھا'
  آپ جس دن دہلی میں رونق افروز ہوتے تو وہ **دو لاکھ ٹنکہ** بطور شکرانہ آپ کی خدمت میں بھیجتا اور جس روز
  دہلی سے روانہ ہوتے **پانچ لاکھ ٹنکہ** پیش کرتا' اس کی درخواست منظور کرکے جب آپ اس سے ملنے جاتے تو وہ
  **تیسرے دروازے پر** آپ کا استقبال کرتا" — Sultan Ala al-Din sent **two lakh tankas** as thanksgiving on
  the day he arrived in Delhi and presented **five lakh tankas** on the day he left; when Rukn al-Din
  granted his request and went to meet him, the sultan received him **at the third gate** (p. 115,
  folio 111). Sultan Qutb al-Din likewise "بہت اعتقاد رکھتا تھا اور آپ کی بہت تعظیم و تکریم کرتا تھا"
  (pp. 115–116, folios 111–112).
- **And the money did not stay.** "جو نذرانہ آتا' آپ اس کو خرچ کر دیتے تھے۔ اس میں سے کچھ نہیں بچاتے
  تھے۔ سلطان علاء الدین جب آپ دہلی آتے **لاکھوں ٹنکے** آپ کو پیش کرتا' آپ وہ سب رقم خرچ کر دیتے تھے" —
  whatever **نذرانہ** came he spent; he saved nothing out of it; when he came to Delhi Sultan Ala al-Din
  presented **lakhs of tankas** and he spent the whole sum (p. 117, folio 113). Read with the preceding
  line: "جو حاجت مند آپ کے پاس آتا' خالی ہاتھ نہیں جاتا' چونکہ آپ لوگوں کی حاجت پوری کرتے تھے۔ اس واسطے
  لوگوں میں آپ **'قبلہ حاجات'** مشہور ہوئے" — no needy person left empty-handed, and for that he became
  famous among the people as the **قبلہ حاجات**, the Qibla of needs (pp. 116–117, folios 112–113).
- **A gift refused at the feet and diverted to a brother.** After food at Nizam al-Din's, "حضرت نظام
  الدین اولیاء رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے خاص خادم **اقبال** نے **ریشمی کپڑوں کے چند تھان اور سو اشرفیاں** ایک
  باریک کپڑے میں رکھ کر آپ کے قدموں پر رکھیں' آپ نے قبول نہیں کیا۔ حضرت محبوب الٰہی نے وہ تھان اور
  اشرفیاں آپ کے بھائی شیخ عماد الدین اسمٰعیل کے سپرد کیں" — Nizam al-Din's special khadim **اقبال** laid
  **several bolts of silk cloth and one hundred ashrafis**, wrapped in a fine cloth, at his feet; he did
  not accept them, and Mahbub-e Ilahi handed them to his brother Imad al-Din Ismail instead (p. 115,
  folio 111, footnote 6 → "اخبار الاخیار (اردو ترجمہ) ص 141"). **A refusal between two shrines that is a
  transfer, not a rejection**, with a named servant carrying the gift and a named relative accepting it.
- **The kulah as an instrument of policy, and an objection from the ranks.** A rich man became a murid
  and repented; Rukn al-Din bestowed a **کلاہ** on him; "ایک درویش نے اس پر اعتراض کیا", a darwish
  objected. The answer: "اے عزیز! اگر وہ بوجہ ایک کلاہ کے گناہ سے باز آجائے اور اس کی **جہت [OCR?]** سے
  بخشا جائے تو میں کس لئے اس کو کلاہ نہ دوں" — if by means of one kulah he gives up sin and is forgiven
  on that account, why should I not give him a kulah (p. 117, folio 113, footnote 13 → "سالک السالکین
  (جلد دوم) ص 523"). **An internal dispute over admitting the wealthy, decided for admission.**
- **Public deportment as a technique.** "کسر نفسی کا یہ عالم تھا کہ جب **پالکی** میں نکلتے تو آپ کے
  دونوں ہاتھ باہر نکلے ہوئے ہوتے" — when he went out in a **پالکی** both his hands hung outside it, so
  that perhaps the hand of some forgiven person might touch his and he too be forgiven (p. 117,
  folio 113). He also took great care of "علماء' فضلاء اور درویشوں", honoured them and spared no pains
  in entertaining them (p. 117, folio 113).
- **A standing food dole, counted.** A devotee "کو آپ کے یہاں سے **چار قرص** ملا کرتے تھے" — used to
  receive **four loaves** from his place; in Mecca he complained that here he got none, and thereafter
  "چار قرض [sic] اور دو پیالے سالن برابر ملتے رہے", **four loaves and two bowls of curry** continued to
  reach him (p. 119, folio 115, footnote 15 → "سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 533"). **The only quantified
  ration attached to this shrine**; on the second spelling see Doubts.
- **A khanqah with cells, and a shrine officer ruling against travel.** "آپ کا ایک مرید **خانقاہ کے
  حجرہ** میں عبادت میں مشغول تھا" — a murid of his was occupied in worship in a **حجرہ** of the khanqah
  (p. 119, folio 115); Rukn al-Din rules that hajj is not obligatory on him "کیوں کہ وہ ایک فقیر ہے",
  because he is a faqir (p. 119, folio 115).
- **Lodging in a madrasa held at his pleasure.** "ملتان میں آپ شیخ رکن الدین ابو الفتح کی اجازت سے مدرسے
  میں رہتے تھے" — in Multan Makhdum Jahaniyan lived in the **مدرسہ** by his **اجازت** (p. 134,
  folio 130). **A shrine shaykh disposing of residence in a teaching institution.**
- **A private boat used to move a student between two shrine towns, and a quarrel settled.** When
  friction ("کشیدگی") arose between Makhdum Jahaniyan's father and Shaikh Jamal al-Din Khandan,
  "انہوں نے آپ کو اپنی خاص کشتی پر سوار کرا کے اونچہ بھجوایا" — he had him boarded on **his own special
  کشتی** and sent to Uch with a message, on which "آپ کے والد اسی وقت حضرت شیخ جمال الدین خنداں کے پاس
  گئے اور ان سے بغل گیر ہوئے" — the father went at once and embraced Jamal al-Din Khandan (p. 134,
  folio 130). **Multan–Uch as a river route, and a boat as shrine property.**
- **He cancels a sultan's appointment.** Makhdum Jahaniyan, holding the منصب شیخ الاسلامی and the مسند
  خانقاہ محمدی at Sehwan, "حضرت شیخ رکن الدین ابو الفتح رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے حکم سے ملازمت سے مستعفی ہو
  گئے" — resigned the post on Rukn al-Din's **حکم** (p. 136, folio 132).
- **He gives khilafat twice over, once awake and once in dream.** The khirqa of khilafat conferred in
  person (p. 135, folio 131), and a khirqa "عالم خواب میں پہنا اور بیدار ہونے کے بعد وہ سر پر پایا" —
  worn in a dream and found on the head on waking (p. 135, folio 131).
- **Two acts of deference recorded to him.** Makhdum Jahaniyan lay down below the "دہلیز" so that he
  could step down, at which "انہوں نے آپ کا ہاتھ پکڑا اور اپنا سینہ آپ کے سینے سے ملایا" (p. 137,
  folio 133, حاشیہ 12 = سیر العارفین); and he bathed in the water running off Rukn al-Din's ghusl, on
  learning of which "آپ کو نعمت خاص سے سرفراز فرمایا" (pp. 137–138, folios 133–134, حاشیہ 13).
- **Relations with Nizam al-Din Auliya**, in numbers: he came "دو بار سلطان علاء الدین کے عہد میں اور
  تین بار سلطان قطب الدین کے عہد میں" — twice in Sultan Ala al-Din's reign and three times in Sultan
  Qutb al-Din's (p. 114, folio 110). In Qutb al-Din's reign Nizam al-Din came out from his khanqah as
  far as "حوض خاص علائی [OCR?]" to receive him, and they then met "اس وقت کی جامع مسجد میں", in the Jami
  Masjid of the time (p. 114, folio 110; repeated p. 116, folio 112). He came from **Multan to Delhi**
  to visit Nizam al-Din in his illness and **led his funeral prayer**, after which "آپ ملتان تشریف لے
  گئے" (p. 115, folio 111). Asked by Sultan Qutb al-Din who had first received him, he answered
  "سب سے پہلے اس شخص نے استقبال کیا' جو اہل شہر میں بہترین شخص ہے", pointing to Nizam al-Din (p. 116,
  folio 112).

**Legends & Miracles**

- **The turban taken at four years old "بطور حق"**, reported with two citations (footnotes 2–3) and
  treated by the book as the designation of the successor (p. 114, folio 110).
- **Baha al-Din Zakariya stands for the unborn child** (p. 113, folio 109, footnote 1 → "سالک السالکین
  (جلد دوم) ص 527").
- **Four loaves delivered in Mecca every Friday night.** A darwish told the stranded devotee "آپ ہر
  جمعہ کی رات کو یہاں آتے ہیں" — that Rukn al-Din comes here every Friday night — and showed him the
  spot; he found and saluted him, and afterwards the ration continued (p. 119, folio 115, footnote 15).
- **The Mughals scattered by an army of angels.** When the Mughals came near Multan and people asked for
  prayers, he did muraqaba and then said "مغل چلے گئے"; reaching the riverbank they dispersed, and asked
  the reason he said "خدا تعالٰی نے **فرشتوں کا لشکر** بھیج دیا اور ان کو منتشر کر دیا" (p. 119,
  folio 115, footnote 16 → "سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 533"). The **دریا** near Multan is named as the
  place of dispersal (p. 119, folio 115).
- **A dream of hajj diagnosed as satanic.** A murid dreamed he was told to go on hajj; Rukn al-Din, on
  seeing him, said at once "وہ خواب **شیطان** کی وجہ سے تھا۔ مشغولی سے محروم کرنا چاہتا ہے" (p. 119,
  folio 115, footnote 17).

**Significance**

- **قبلہ حاجات** — the name the people gave him because no needy person left empty-handed (pp. 116–117,
  folios 112–113).
- **مرجع خاص و عام** — the standing of the Multan mazar (p. 116, folio 112).
- **A shrine treated as a conduit rather than an endowment: cash in, cash out, no accumulation stated**
  (p. 117, folio 113).
- The one order-bearing statement about him in this range is the inherited **خرقہ** of Shaikh Shihab
  al-Din Suhrawardi (p. 114, folio 110); **the book never calls him Suhrawardi** and names no silsila
  for him (p. 113, folio 109; p. 116, folio 112; and nowhere in باب 22).

### shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht — حضرت سید جلال الدین بخاری، مخدوم جہانیاں جہاں گشت (باب 22)

Subject of باب 22 (pp. 133–142, folios 129–138). He is also named, without being mapped by the chunk
notes, at four other points in this range: as a khalifa of Rukn al-Din (p. 116, folio 112), as the pir
of Shaikh Qawam al-Din (p. 183, folio 179), as the grandfather of Qutb Alam (p. 190, folio 186), as the
pir of Shah Ajmal (p. 191, folio 187) and as the ancestor of Shah Alam (p. 195, folio 191). **Those
mentions are recorded below and under Doubts with their unmapped status intact; they are not merged
into the entry as if the identification were settled.**

**Basis of the identification, from باب 22 alone.** "آپ کی ولادت باسعادت اوچہ میں 15' شعبان 707ھ کو
واقع ہوئی" — born at **اوچہ (Uch)** on 15 Sha'ban 707 AH (p. 133, folio 129, حاشیہ 1 = سالک السالکین
(جلد دوم) ص 425 and اخبار الاخیار (اردو ترجمہ) ص 498); "آپ اپنے والد ماجد سے سلسلہ سہروردی میں بیعت
ہوئے" — he took bai'at from his father **in the سہروردی silsila** (p. 135, folio 131); and the laqab
"مخدوم جہانیاں جہاں گشت" is the book's own (p. 133, folio 129). Birth-town, order and title together
agree with the row.

**Saint**

- Opening characterisation: "حضرت سید جلال الدین بخاری **سیاح بادیہ تجرید [OCR?]** و تفرید ہیں" —
  traveller of the wilderness of tajrid[OCR?] and tafrid (p. 133, folio 129).
- **Family and town of origin**: "آپ سادات حسینی سے ہیں۔ بخارا کے ایک معزز خاندان سے تھے" — of the
  Husaini sayyids, of a respected family of **بخارا** (p. 133, folio 129). Father **سید احمد کبیر**
  (p. 133, folio 129); grandfather **شیخ جلال الدین سرخ بخاری** (p. 133, folio 129); younger brother
  **سید صدر الدین المعروف بہ راجو قتال** (p. 137, folio 133).
- **Names and titles**: "آپ کا نام سید جلال الدین حسین ہے"; kunya "**ابو عبداللہ**"; laqab "**مخدوم
  جہانیاں جہاں گشت**", and "بعض آپ کو "مخدوم جہانیاں" کے لقب سے پکارتے ہیں" — some call him simply
  Makhdum Jahaniyan (p. 133, folio 129).
- **The laqab derived from three mazars in one night.** On an Eid night he kept vigil at Baha al-Din
  Zakariya's mazar, then presented himself at the mazars of "صدر الدین عارف اور [OCR?] حضرت رکن الدین
  ابو الفتح"; "ہر جگہ عیدی مانگی' ہر جگہ سے آپ کو "مخدوم جہانیاں" کا خطاب عطا ہوا" — he asked for
  **عیدی** at each place and from each the title Makhdum Jahaniyan was conferred (pp. 133–134,
  folios 129–130, حاشیہ 2). **The town of the three mazars is (not stated) on that page.**
- **Schooling**: initial education at "اونچہ" under "علامہ قاضی بہاء الدین"; after the qazi's death he
  came from Uch to **ملتان** to finish "ہدایہ و بزدوی" under "شیخ موسیٰ اور مولانا مجد الدین"; then
  "مدینہ جا کر مزید تعلیم حاصل کی" (p. 134, folio 130).
- **Bai'at, khirqas and transmitters.** Bai'at from his father in the Suhrawardi silsila (p. 135,
  folio 131); "پھر آپ نے اپنے چچا حضرت سید صدر الدین المعروف بہ محمد غوث سے خرقہ پہنا۔ حضرت شیخ رکن
  الدین ابو الفتح رحمۃ اللہ علیہ سے بھی آپ نے خرقہ خلافت پہنا" (p. 135, folio 131); "آپ نے چودہ
  خانوادوں میں بیعت اور خلافت حاصل کی" — bai'at and khilafat in **fourteen خانوادے** (p. 135, folio 131,
  حاشیہ 4). He claims the "خرقہ سیادت حضرت علی کرم اللہ وجہہ سے… کہ جو خرقہ ان کو سرور عالم حضرت محمد
  مصطفیٰ صلی اللہ علیہ وسلم سے ملا تھا" (p. 135, folio 131), and wore Baha al-Din Zakariya's khirqa
  received from his father (p. 135, folio 131). **Two khirqas received in dream**, from Rukn al-Din Abu
  al-Fath and from Nizam al-Din Auliya, each found on his head on waking (p. 135, folio 131). A further
  khirqa of khilafat from Nasir al-Din Chiragh Dehlvi on his return (p. 137, folio 133). The list of
  transmitters, given without narrative (pp. 135–136, folios 131–132, حاشیہ 5): حضرت شیخ قوام الدین
  (khalifa of Rukn al-Din Abu al-Fath), حضرت قطب الدین منور، حضرت نصیرالدین چراغ دہلوی، شیخ مکہ امام
  عبداللہ یافعی، شیخ مدینہ حضرت عبداللہ المطری، قطب عدن حضرت **فقنیمہ بصال [OCR?]**، شیخ اسحاق کازرونی،
  شیخ امین الدین، "شیخ امین الدین' بھائی شیخ امام الدین"، شیخ حمید الدین، "حضرت شیخ شہاب الدین سہروردی
  رحمۃ اللہ علیہ کے خلیفہ **شیخ شرف الدین محمود شاہ تشتری [OCR?]**"، سید احمد رفاعی، شیخ نجم الدین
  صنعانی، حضرت شیخ نجم الدین کبریٰ، حضرت خضر، **حضرت احد الدین حنی [OCR?]**، حضرت شیخ نور الدین.
- **The Hijaz years, with a return date**: "چھ سال مدینہ اور مکہ میں رہ کر 752ھ میں وطن واپس ہوئے" —
  after six years in Medina and Mecca he returned home in **752 AH** (p. 137, folio 133, حاشیہ 7 = سالک
  السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 429'432); on that journey he met "**سید صدر الدین کماش [OCR?]**" and "**شیخ حمید
  الدین بن محمود الحنی [OCR?] سمرقندی**" (p. 137, folio 133, حاشیہ 8).
- **Marriage**: "آپ نے شادی کی تھی" — he married; **no wife is named, no number of children is given**
  (p. 137, folio 133). A four-year-old youngest son appears in a karamat (pp. 140–141, folios 136–137).
- **Death, as printed**: "کچھ روز بیمار رہ کر 10' ذی الحجہ 788ھ کو آپ رحمت حق میں پیوست ہوئے" — after
  some days' illness he passed into God's mercy on **10 Zi al-Hijja 788 AH** (p. 137, folio 133,
  حاشیہ 10 = سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 426). **The archive row carries 1384 CE; both values stand here
  side by side and no conversion has been made** (see Doubts).
- **Devotional regime, quantified**: "دن و رات میں پانسو رکعت نماز پڑھنا آپ کا معمول تھا۔ آپ ساتوں قرات
  کے قاری تھے۔ آخری عمر میں بھی سو رکعت نماز پڑھتے تھے۔ ذکر جہر بہت کرتے تھے۔ لباس آپ کا معمولی تھا" —
  **five hundred rakats** by day and night, a reciter in all **seven qirats**, still **a hundred rakats**
  in his last years, much **ذکر جہر**, plain dress (p. 137, folio 133).
- **Sama, with the book's own contradiction attached**: "سماع سنتے تھے۔ بعض کہتے ہیں کہ سماع نہیں سنتے
  تھے" — he listened to sama; some say he did not (p. 137, folio 133, حاشیہ 11 = سالک السالکین (جلد
  دوم) ص 435). **Both clauses stand; the book does not choose.**
- **Humility about the turban**: "آپ میں انکساری اس درجہ تھی کہ ٹوپی کے ساتھ نماز پڑھتے تھے' آپ فرماتے
  تھے کہ عمامہ خاص مردوں کے لئے ہے اور میں مرد نہیں ہوں" — he prayed in a **ٹوپی**, saying the **عمامہ**
  is for men and "I am not a man" (p. 138, folio 134).
- **Teachings** under named headings — خرقہ, صبر, تقویٰ, مشیخیت, شریعت طریقت حقیقت — plus a twenty-item
  اقوال list and a ورد و وظیفہ (pp. 138–140, folios 134–136).

**Shrine / Darbar**

- **Mazar: (not stated). Urs: (not stated). Sajjada nashin at a shrine: (not stated). Endowment: (not
  stated).** The وفات rubric gives the date only; **the chunk never locates his grave** (p. 137,
  folio 133).
- What supports the row's Uch Sharif is residence and movement, not burial: **birth at اوچہ** (p. 133,
  folio 129), **i'tikaf in the Jami Masjid of اوچہ** (p. 141, folio 137) and journeys "اوچہ سے دہلی"
  (p. 137, folio 133).

**Practices & Events**

- **A state religious office attached to a named khanqah, held in a named jurisdiction, then resigned on
  a shaykh's order.** "سلطان محمد تغلق شاہ کے عہد میں آپ کو منصب شیخ الاسلامی و مسند خانقاہ محمدی پیش
  کیا گیا۔ آپ نے عہدہ قبول فرمایا۔ کچھ عرصے تک منصب شیخ الاسلامی و مسند خانقاہ محمدی پر سیوستان اور اس
  کے مضافات میں فائز رہ کر اپنے فرائض بحسن و خوبی انجام دیتے رہے" — the **منصب شیخ الاسلامی** and the
  **مسند خانقاہ محمدی** were offered to him; he accepted and held them for a time at **سیوستان** and its
  **مضافات**; then he resigned on Rukn al-Din Abu al-Fath's order (p. 136, folio 132). **A salaried state
  office, a named khanqah as its seat, a named provincial jurisdiction, and a shrine shaykh's veto over
  it.**
- **A minister's offering accepted and redirected to the victim, with a count of visits.** Khan Jahan,
  Firoz's wazir, had imprisoned the son of a **محرر**; Makhdum Jahaniyan "انیس بار سفارش کرنے تشریف لے
  گئے" — went **nineteen times** to intercede and was not received; on the twentieth Khan Jahan sent word
  "اے سید تم کو غیرت نہیں آتی", to which he replied "اے عزیز! میں جتنی بار آتا ہوں' مجھ کو ثواب ملتا
  ہے۔ میں چاہتا ہوں کہ مظلوم کو تیرے ہاتھ سے چھڑاؤں' تاکہ تو بھی درجہ ثواب کو پہنچے"; Khan Jahan came
  "نگے سر' گلے میں رسی ڈالے ہوئے", sought pardon, became a murid, "محرر کے بیٹے کو خلعت اور گھوڑا دے کر
  رہا کر دیا", and presented a **نذرانہ** — "آپ نے وہ نذرانہ محرر کے بیٹے کو دے دیا", which he gave to
  the clerk's son (p. 138, folio 134, حاشیہ 14 = سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 434). A **خلعت**, a horse and
  a redirected nazrana.
- **Royal devotion and a route.** "سلطان فیروز تغلق آپ کا معتقد و منقاد تھا۔ اس کے عہد حکومت میں آپ کئی
  دفعہ اوچہ سے دہلی تشریف لائے" — Sultan Firoz Tughluq was his devotee and obedient to him, and in his
  reign he came **several times from Uch to Delhi** (p. 137, folio 133, حاشیہ 9 = اخبار الاخیار (اردو
  ترجمہ) ص 97).
- **A Jami Masjid cleared by the town's governor during his i'tikaf.** "ایک سال ماہ رمضان میں آپ نے اوچہ
  کی جامع مسجد میں اعتکاف فرمایا۔ ایک دن اوچہ کا حاکم جس کا نام سومرہ تھا' جامع مسجد میں آیا۔ آپ کے پاس
  بہت لوگ جمع ہو گئے' اس نے سب کو جامع مسجد سے باہر نکال دیا" — in a Ramadan he kept **اعتکاف** in the
  **جامع مسجد of اوچہ**; the **حاکم of Uch, named سومرہ**, came in and **turned everyone out** where a
  crowd had gathered around him (p. 141, folio 137, حاشیہ 16). **A named local officer dispersing a
  shaykh's congregation in the congregational mosque of the shrine town.**
- **A mazar prescribed as the site of a cure, with a named three-step procedure.** After Sumra was driven
  mad, his mother brought him and sought pardon; "آپ نے اس کو ہدایت فرمائی کہ سومرہ کو غسل دے کر اور
  کپڑے پہنا کر حضرت جمال الدین خنداں کے مزار پر لے جائے۔ اور پھر آپ کے پاس لائے" — **bathe him, clothe
  him, take him to the mazar of Jamal al-Din Khandan, and then bring him back**; "اس کی ماں نے ایسا ہی
  کیا", and after his apology "آپ کی دعا سے وہ بالکل اچھا ہو گیا" (p. 141, folio 137, حاشیہ 16). **A
  therapeutic circuit running through a third saint's tomb** — the clearest ritual prescription attached
  to this figure. **The town of Jamal al-Din Khandan's mazar is (not stated)** (p. 141, folio 137).
- **His own account of what initiation is.** "آپ جب کسی کو مرید کرتے تو فرماتے کہ "میں صرف وکیل ہوں"" —
  whenever he made someone a murid he would say **"I am only a وکیل"** (p. 138, folio 134).
- **Two kinds of khirqa distinguished, one of them explicitly a relic.** "خرقہ دو طرح کا ہے۔ خرقہ تصوف
  اور خرقہ تشبہ خرقہ تصوف خرقہ صحبت ہے اور خرقہ تشبہ خرقہ تبرک کو کہتے ہیں' اس میں صحبت کی شرط نہیں" —
  the **خرقہ تصوف** is the robe of **صحبت**; the **خرقہ تشبہ** is the **خرقہ تبرک**, **in which
  companionship is not a condition** (p. 138, folio 134). **The book's own doctrinal warrant for
  relic-robes handed out without discipleship** — and it sits nine pages after the list of the eighteen
  figures from whom he himself took khirqa, two of them in dreams (pp. 135–136, folios 131–132). The book
  does not draw the connection.
- **Three conditions laid down for being a shaykh at all.** "مشیخیت کے لئے تین شرطیں ہیں۔ اگر یہ تینوں
  نہ ہوں تو مشیخیت درست نہیں۔ اول شرط یہ ہے کہ وہ تین علم یعنی شریعت' و حقیقت کا عالم ہو۔ دوسری شرط یہ
  ہے کہ اس زمانے کے علماء اس کو قبول کریں اور اس کے معتقد مرید ہوں۔ تیسری شرط یہ ہے کہ اس کو سوائے حق
  تعالیٰ کے اور کوئی طلب نہ ہو" (p. 139, folio 135). **The second condition makes scholarly recognition
  constitutive of shrine authority.** The list promises three sciences and names two — see Doubts.
- **A named daily wird**: "یاحیی یاقیوم اسم اعظم ہے" (p. 140, folio 136).
- **Deference at Medina**: leading the prayer in the Prophet's mosque in the imam's absence, "آپ بپاس
  ادب اس جگہ سے جہاں سرور عالم صلی اللہ علیہ وسلم کھڑے ہوتے تھے۔ ایک صف پیچھے کھڑے ہوئے" — he stood
  **one row behind** the Prophet's standing-place (p. 136, folio 132).
- **His own khalifas, two named**: "قاضی عبدالمالک المعروف بہ شاہ اجل اور سید صدر الدین المعروف بہ راجو
  قتال جو آپ کے چھوٹے بھائی ہیں" (p. 137, folio 133). A third, **حضرت شیخ قوام الدین**, is attributed to
  him at p. 183 (folio 179): "جو حضرت مخدوم جہانیاں کے مرید اور خلیفہ ہیں' اپنے زمانے کے مشہور بزرگ
  تھے" — **recorded here with its unmapped status; that page gives him no town, no mazar, no death year
  and no silsila** (p. 183, folio 179).
- **Later attributions to him in this range, all recorded unmapped by the chunk notes**: Shah Ajmal was
  his murid and khalifa "سلسلہ چشتیہ جمانیہ[OCR?] و قادریہ و سہروردیہ میں" (p. 191, folio 187); Qutb
  Alam is "مخدوم جہانیاں جہاں گشت حضرت سید جلال الدین بخاری کے پوتے", his grandson (p. 190, folio 186);
  Shah Alam is the great-grandson of "مخدوم جہانیاں جہاں گشت کے بیٹے حضرت سید جلال الدین بخاری", the
  **son** of Makhdoom Jahaniyan (p. 195, folio 191). **باب 32/33 and باب 35 contradict each other on
  whether "سید جلال الدین بخاری" is Makhdoom Jahaniyan himself or his son; both readings stand** (see
  Doubts).

**Legends & Miracles**

- **A child's death foretold from a distraction in prayer.** His four-year-old youngest son played near
  his musalla during the **چاشت** prayer and broke his concentration; afterwards he told "سید شمس
  الدین": "مجھے اس لڑکے کا زندہ رہنا مشکل نظر آتا ہے' کیوں کہ عین نماز میں میری طبیعت اس کی طرف مائل
  تھی"; "ظہر کے وقت اس لڑکے کو بخار ہوا اور اسی رات اس کا انتقال ہو گیا" (pp. 140–141, folios 136–137).
- **Rain prayed for, then prayed away**, on the authority of تاریخ فرشتہ (فارسی) ص 416: in Delhi people
  distressed by drought sought his prayer; "اتنی بارش ہوئی کہ لوگوں نے عاجز ہو کر بارش کے بند ہونے کی آپ
  سے دعا کرائی' آپ نے دعا کی اور بارش رک گئی" (p. 141, folio 137).
- **A governor driven mad and restored**, on the authority of سالک السالکین (جلد دوم) ص 436: at the words
  "اے سومرہ! کیا تو دیوانہ ہو گیا ہے کہ درویشوں کو تنگ کرتا ہے", "آپ کا یہ فرمانا تھا کہ سومرہ دیوانہ ہو
  گیا"; after the mazar circuit and his apology, "آپ کی دعا سے وہ بالکل اچھا ہو گیا" (p. 141, folio 137).
- **A gift absorbed by embrace, then explained away by the book.** "آپ کے متعلق یہ کہا جاتا ہے کہ… آپ جب
  کبھی کسی سے معانقہ فرماتے تو جو نعمت اس کے پاس ہوتی' اسی وقت جذب کر لیتے", glossed by the book's own
  "یعنی" as "آپ اس قدر توجہ اور خدمت سے کام لیتے کہ وہ شخص بے اختیار ہو کر آپ کو اپنی ہر نعمت دے دیتا"
  (pp. 134–135, folios 130–131, حاشیہ 3 = اخبار الاخیار (اردو ترجمہ) ص 296).
- **A greeting answered from the grave at Medina**: he said "السلام علیک یا جدی" and "وہاں سے آپ کو جواب
  ملا: "وعلیک السلام یا ولدی"" (p. 136, folio 132, حاشیہ 6 = سیر العارفین).
- **Dates eaten with their stones.** At seven, given "چھوارے" from a "خوان" before Shaikh Jamal al-Din
  Khandan, he ate them "مع گٹھلیوں کے" and explained "اس میں بھی برکت ہے اور پھینکنا بے ادبی ہے"
  (p. 134, folio 130).
- **He is acted upon in a neighbouring entry.** The Panipat saint reached **دہلی** "بقوت روحانی ایک ساعت
  میں", found him "حالت نزع میں", prayed for him and "اپنی عمر کے چند سال ان کو دے کر" gave him some
  years of his own life, returning to Panipat in a moment (p. 131, folio 127); the same event is told
  once earlier as Firoz Shah's information (p. 129, folio 125, حاشیہ 7). **The two tellings differ by one
  letter — صحبت on p. 129 (folio 125) against صحت on p. 131 (folio 127) — and neither is OCR-flagged**
  (see Doubts). And Ashraf Jahangir Simnani, reaching **اوچ**, "حضرت مخدوم جہانیاں جہاں گشت کے روحانی
  فیوض و برکات سے مستفید و مستفیض ہوئے" (p. 146, folio 142).

**Significance**

- The one entry in this range whose subject is an archive row in its own right, and the range's densest
  single account of a shrine figure holding, and being made to give up, a state office (p. 136,
  folio 132).
- **Spelling of the town varies three ways within ten pages**: "اوچہ" (pp. 133, 137, 141; folios 129,
  133, 137), "اونچہ" (p. 134, folio 130), "اوچ" (p. 146, folio 142).

### garh-maharaja-shorkot — حضرت سلطان باہو (باب 60, pp. 313–321, folios 309–317)
